- Tests added: `SupportWriteAuditInterceptorTest` (3: each write method answered 2xx, 204 included, goes to the auditor with its route, path, status and start; GET/HEAD, 409, 500, an escaped exception, a replayed key, async and error dispatches, the club's own admin and no user write nothing; an auditor failure is swallowed and a second completion records nothing more); `SupportWriteAuditTest` (3: the entry's action, entity, origin, role, account, trace and details; a request that already left a support entry gets none; no `route` key when Spring matched none); `SupportAccessIT.AUTHZ_05_everyWriteOfASupportAccessLeavesAnAuditEntryOfThePlatformTeam` (`@AuditCovers(SUPPORT_WRITE)`, over HTTP: a saved view created and deleted with the support token leaves two `SUPPORT_WRITE` entries with their details, the replay of its key, a refused duplicate (409) and a read leave none, an audited `PUT /parameters/{key}` keeps only its `PARAMETER_CHANGED` with `PLATFORM_SUPPORT`, the club admin's own saved view leaves none, «Surt» (`POST /oauth2/revoke`) leaves one; the club's `GET /audit-entries?filter=action:eq:SUPPORT_WRITE` lists the three, the console's lists none, the other club has none). Earlier commit: `ParameterCatalogContractTest.AUTHZ_05_theSupportAccessCeilingOfTheDocumentIsTheCatalogMaximum`, `SupportAccessIT.AUTHZ_05_aSupportAccessNeverLastsMoreThanSixtyMinutes`.
- Run and passing: unit `SupportWriteAuditInterceptorTest` (3), `SupportWriteAuditTest` (3), `AuditContractTest` (1, every action covered), `AuditWriterTest` (6), `AuditedAspectTest` (6), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `ArchitectureRulesTest` (23), `ParameterCatalogContractTest` (6); ITs `SupportAccessIT` (10), `E2ContractIT` (8, the S14 R-14-09 action row equals `AuditActionName`), `AuditedAspectIT` (15), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, via `bin/openapi-snapshot`). No existing test changed.
- Assumption: «write» is read as an HTTP `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` that its handler answered 2xx (the conservative reading for an audit duty: a `POST` that only computes, such as `POST /billing/simulations`, is recorded too; a refused or failed request changed nothing and a replayed key runs no handler). The `SUPPORT_WRITE` entry is written when the request ends, outside the change's transaction (many unaudited writes have none); if that insert fails the write stands and the miss is logged, the same trade-off as an answered request. «Surt» (`/oauth2/revoke`) is a write of the token and is recorded. The maximum is the product default, 60 minutes, which S17 R-17-10 already names («S01, 60 min»): the console can shorten an access, never lengthen it.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md `platform.supportAccessMinutes`: **màxim 60** and a paragraph «Sostre de l'accés de suport» (refused above, a stored override above is ignored).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S17-consola-de-clubs.md R-17-10: the access lasts `platform.supportAccessMinutes`, 60 minutes at most; every write of the token (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE answered 2xx) leaves an entry with `origin = PLATFORM_SUPPORT`, its service's own or `SUPPORT_WRITE`, also «Surt»; a read, a refusal or a replay none; permissions unchanged. §9 names the maximum. Two Canvis lines of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S14-tauler-auditoria-exportacions-rgpd.md R-14-09 (the audit-action catalogue): new action `SUPPORT_WRITE` (`entityType = ImpersonationGrant`, `details {method, route, path, status}`, written when the request ends, club audit only); R-14-10: `SUPPORT_WRITE` is an «event» action; Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated) `AuditAction` enum + `SUPPORT_WRITE`; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry «AUTHZ-05».
- OTHER-REPO: web. Adopt the regenerated OpenAPI snapshot (`packages/api-client/openapi/openapi.json` and the generated `schema.d.ts`: `AuditAction` gains `SUPPORT_WRITE`). Add `admin-audit:actions.SUPPORT_WRITE` in `packages/i18n/src/locales/{ca,es,en}/admin-audit.json` (proposed: «Canvi fet pel suport» · «Cambio hecho por soporte» · «Change made by support»), and show its `details.method` and `details.route` (or `path`) on the D9 row, as the other entries show their details. No route, role or error change.
- Review note (review of p2-api-06-access-limits, 14 Nov 2026): accepted; the owner should know four things. (1) The decision named no value for the maximum. The implementation uses 60, which is also the product default, so the console can now only shorten an access; before this change it could lengthen one. A longer intervention needs a new access with its own reason. (2) Outside an `Idempotency-Key` request, `SUPPORT_WRITE` is written after the answer and outside the write's transaction. If that insert fails, the write stands and only the error log `SupportWriteAuditFailed` records the miss. «Every write» is therefore met except when the audit store fails. The only way to close that gap is to write the entry inside each service's transaction. (3) A «write» means a `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` answered 2xx. A `GET` with a side effect is not recorded, and a `POST` that only computes is. (4) Until the web adds the `SUPPORT_WRITE` label (OTHER-REPO above), D9 lists these rows under the raw action key. Reviewed: the trace id comes from the server, not from a request header, so a support user cannot reuse one to hide a write. The details hold only the method, the route pattern, the path (ids only) and the status, never the body, the query or the token. Unit tests (interceptor, audit, contract, ArchUnit) and the ITs `SupportAccessIT` (10), `E2ContractIT` (8), `AuditedAspectIT` (15), `SecurityInventoryIT` (4) and `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12) were re-run and passed.

### AUTHZ-06 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. `POST /api/v1/signup/identity-checks` is anonymous (`SecurityConfiguration.java:64-66`, `SignupController.java:140-141`). It answers `NEW`, `SIGNUP_ALREADY_PENDING`, `CONTACT_CLUB` or `VERIFICATION_SENT` with the masked access e-mail, and `VERIFICATION_SENT` emits `SignupRecognitionRequested`, which e-mails N-39 to the member (`SignupService.java:201-213`). S04 R-04-05 prescribes exactly these results. Bucket `SIGNUP_IDENTITY`: 10 per hour per club and IP (`signup.rateLimit.identityChecksPerHour`, `editableBy: PLATFORM`); N-39 is capped at 3 per recipient per hour.
- To decide (an S04 change, plus web screen 16): (a) keep R-04-05; (b) give one uniform answer whether or not anyone matched («if we know you, we have e-mailed you»), and move the pending and contact-the-club outcomes to the e-mail or to the final `POST /signup`; (c) lower the bucket.
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): keep `signup.enabled=false` (a club parameter) until the launch communication is out, or accept that members may receive N-39 before it.

### AUTHZ-07 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. Role changes go through `RoleAssignmentService.apply` → `TeamMembershipService.save` (`RoleAssignmentService.java:100-105`) and leave `accessRevokedAt` alone. Its only writers are `AccountRepository.revokeAll` (`:113-115`, reached only from erasure through `TokenService.revokeAll`) and `IdentityErasureRepository`. A refresh re-reads the membership (`TokenService.refresh`), so the old roles last until the access token expires (`ACCESS_TTL` 15 min, `TokenService.java:31`). S05 R-05-11 prescribes this («el JWT vigent (15 min) caduca sol»), and S01 R-01-10 accepts the same window for a global revocation.
- To decide (an S05/S01 change): (a) keep the 15-minute window; (b) call `TokenService.revokeAll` when a role is removed: immediate, but it signs the person out on every device and in every club, because `accessRevokedAt` belongs to the account; (c) add a per-membership marker (e.g. `rolesChangedAt`) that `AccountStateFilter` compares with the token's `iat` for that club only, so the app refreshes into the new roles (a new field and one more read per request); (d) shorten the access token's lifetime.
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): no API path ends a live access token after a role removal. Treat a removed administrator or instructor as active for 15 more minutes.

## api-04-identity-auth

### AUTH-01 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. There is no second factor or step-up (no TOTP/WebAuthn code; `auth_time` is read only by the OIDC flow). `clubs-admin` accepts the password and magic-link grants (`application.yml:164-170`), the password floor is `auth.passwordMinLength` 8, and each refresh slides to `now + auth.sessionDays` (30) with no absolute cap (`TokenService.java:254`), the same for admins and members. The sliding 30 days is what S01 R-01-06 and ADR-010 prescribe. A support token is narrower than a club admin's (exports, SEPA files and impersonation are closed to it, `SupportAccessService.java:51-58`), but a platform admin's own session reaches the console.
- To decide: (a) a second factor (TOTP or passkeys) for ADMIN and AGILITYHUB_ADMIN, or as an interim a step-up (a password login within N minutes, from `auth_time`) on exports, remittance files, bank-account and payment-provider changes (a new error code in the closed catalogue, web prompts); (b) an absolute lifetime for `clubs-admin` sessions: `auth.sessionDays` is per club, not per client or role, so this means a new parameter and an R-01-06 change; (c) a higher password minimum for staff (see AUTH-08); (d) an e-mail to the admin on every new back-office session (a new notification in the closed catalogue).
- NOT-CODE: query production for accounts created by `club:apply` (`createdSource: CONSOLE`), in particular the two seed ADMIN accounts of `seeds/club-canic.yaml:74-85` that share `SEED_PASSWORD`, and remove or block them along with the shared QA administrator. Lowering `auth.sessionDays` from the console is possible today, but it also shortens member sessions.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): step-up for exports, remittance file downloads, bank-account changes and payment-provider changes (a recent password confirmation: re-enter the password when the sign-in is not recent, or a fresh sign-in link for an account without a password); back-office sessions end at 12 hours; a 12-character minimum when a staff account (ADMIN, INSTRUCTOR, PLATFORM) sets or changes its password, members keep 8; no second factor.
- Sessions (commit bfe14ab): `TokenService.BACK_OFFICE_SESSION` = 12 h, a technical constant. A `clubs-admin` session (a club's back office and the console) ends 12 hours after its family's `createdAt` (the password, link or handoff sign-in that opened it): a refresh caps the refresh token's `expiresAt`, the `ah_refresh` cookie's `Max-Age` and the access token's `exp` at that end, and afterwards answers `400 REFRESH_EXPIRED` (`TokenService.ended`), also for a family issued before the change with a later `expiresAt`. An ended family is not listed by `/me/sessions`, not counted for `auth.maxSessions`, and not usable for a handoff, a profile switch or a password change (`requireFamily`). `clubs-app`, `id-web` and the BODY clients keep the sliding `auth.sessionDays`.
- Passwords (commit dc4f742): `PasswordService.STAFF_PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH` = 12. `PUT /me/password` (setting a first password or changing it) needs 12 code points, or `auth.passwordMinLength` when that is higher, for an account with ADMIN or INSTRUCTOR in any club membership that is not ERASED (`AccountMembershipRepository.holdsStaffRole`) or with AGILITYHUB_ADMIN; members keep `auth.passwordMinLength` (8). `400 PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT` now carries `details.minLength`. Existing passwords stay valid.
- Step-up: new `@RecentLogin` and `RecentLogins` (`shared.application`) and `shared.api.RecentLoginAspect`. A staff caller (authority ADMIN, INSTRUCTOR or AGILITYHUB_ADMIN) reaches an annotated handler only when its token's `auth_time` is at most 15 minutes old (`RecentLogins.WINDOW`); otherwise, or without `auth_time`, `403 RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED {maxAgeMinutes: 15}` (new code, `ErrorCode` and messages ca/es/en). The aspect (order 300) runs inside `@PreAuthorize` (200), so a caller without the route's role keeps `403 FORBIDDEN`, and before the audit aspect's write and the handler; the MVC interceptors (tenant, module, support, impersonation) answer first as before. The 24 annotated operations (the inventory is `RecentLoginRoutesTest.EXPECTED`): `GET`/`POST /members/export`, `/dogs/export`, `/audit-entries/export`; `GET /activities/export`, `/activity-registrations/export`, `/invoices/export`, `/notifications/export`, `/training-bookings/export`, `/instructor/week/export`, `/billing/exports`; `GET /exports`, `/exports/{id}`, `/exports/{id}/download`; `POST /members/{id}/data-export`, `/me/data-export`; `GET /platform/clubs/{clubId}/migrations/{runId}/report`; `GET /remittances/{id}/file`, `/remittances/{id}/spreadsheet`; `PATCH /members/{id}/payment-method`; `PUT`/`DELETE /platform/clubs/{id}/payment-providers/{provider}`. `PATCH /members/{id}` asks for it in `MembersController.updateMember` when its body carries `paymentMethod` (a pending member's bank account), before its transaction. The sign-in time was already in every access token (`auth_time`: password grant, link, OIDC; a refresh and a profile switch keep it). Two changes keep it honest: a handoff now carries the source session's sign-in time into the new session (`HandoffService.signedIn` → `TokenService.handoff(…, authTime)`, both exchange paths), so an app switch is no fresh sign-in; a support token carries the `auth_time` of the console session that started it (`SupportAccessService.start`), none when that token has none. Signing in again is the existing password grant or `LOGIN` link: no route, role, parameter, event, notification or audit action added. `OpenApiConfiguration.declareRecentLogin` adds the code to each annotated operation's 403; OpenAPI snapshot regenerated with `bin/openapi-snapshot` (26 changed lines: the 24 operations, plus the 403 and the description of `PATCH /members/{id}`); `docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md` entry «AUTH-01 (c)».
- Tests added (sessions and passwords, commits bfe14ab and dc4f742): `BackOfficeSessionTest` (6 unit cases: the 12-hour end for `clubs-admin` and none for other clients; a refresh never slides past it and its access token ends with it; the member app slides its 30 days with 15-minute access tokens; a session past 12 hours is not renewed even with a longer stored expiry; an ended session is not usable nor listed; a sign-in gets 12 hours); `BackOfficeSessionIT` (3: refreshed every few hours, the session ends at 12 h with `REFRESH_EXPIRED`; the member app keeps 30 days; a session issued before the cap ends at its next refresh); `PasswordServiceSurvivorsTest` (4 new cases: club staff and platform administrators need 12, members keep the club minimum with `details.minLength`, a higher club minimum still applies to staff); `StaffPasswordIT` (3: a club ADMIN needs 12, a member keeps 8, a staff role in any club counts but an ERASED membership does not).
- Tests added (step-up): `RecentLoginsTest` (5 unit cases: 15 minutes for ADMIN, INSTRUCTOR and AGILITYHUB_ADMIN, refused one second later with `maxAgeMinutes: 15`; no `auth_time` is never recent; a member is never asked; only a bearer token has a sign-in; the claim's three decoded forms); `RecentLoginRoutesTest` (2: the annotated operations are exactly the inventory; every bearer export, remittance or migration file route that S17 R-17-10 closes to support carries the annotation, so a new export cannot miss it); `SupportAccessRecentLoginTest` (2: a support token carries its console session's `auth_time`, and none without one); `StepUpIT` (5: an ADMIN signed in 16 minutes ago and refreshed is refused on the census export, a member data export, `/exports`, `/exports/{id}`, the remittance file and spreadsheet while `GET /members` still works, and a new password sign-in opens them again; the bank account through `/payment-method` and through `PATCH /members/{id}` with `paymentMethod`, but not a `PATCH` without it; a handoff from a fresh app session opens the export and one from a 16-minute-old session does not; an INSTRUCTOR keeps `403 FORBIDDEN` on the census export and is refused on its week PDF, a member is never asked; `PUT`/`DELETE` of a payment provider with an `auth_time` of 16 minutes is refused and of 1 minute is not).
- Tests changed to the decided behaviour: `CookieRefreshIT` (commit bfe14ab) expects `Max-Age=43200` for `clubs-admin` and keeps 2592000 for the others; `IdentityCoreIT` (commit dc4f742) takes the staff minimum for its ADMIN account. The synthetic staff tokens that ITs send to the 24 routes now carry a fresh `auth_time` (the new `AbstractIntegrationTest.signedInNow()`), as a session opened by a sign-in does, in the token helpers of `BillingItSupport`, `PrivacyItFixtures`, `BookingFixtures`, `ActivityFixtures`, `TrainingFixtures`, `CensusIT`, `ExportEngineIT`, `UniversalListsIT`, `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT`, `SignupSecurityFixesIT`, `NotificationLogIT`, `AuditQueriesIT`, `ConsoleProviderLockIT`, `PlatformConsoleIT`, `SupportAccessIT` (its console token), `MigrationReportsIT` and the E2, E4, E5, E6, E7, E8, E10 and list-fields contract ITs; no assertion changed. `StepUpIT` covers the refusal. `S04ErrorContractTest` follows the handlers' calls through a slice of classes: the slice gains `shared.application.RecentLogins`, which `PATCH /members/{id}` now calls, so the code that route documents is reached (its first run failed with «documents RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED but its handler cannot throw it»).
- Run and passing: unit `RecentLoginsTest` (5), `RecentLoginRoutesTest` (2), `SupportAccessRecentLoginTest` (2), `SupportAccessServiceSurvivors3Test` (2), `BackOfficeSessionTest` (6), `TokenServiceSurvivorsTest` (20), `PasswordServiceSurvivorsTest` (9), `HandoffServiceAppSwitchTest` (2), `HandoffServiceSurvivorsTest` (1), `ErrorCatalogContractTest` (7), `ErrorMessagesRegisterTest` (2), `MessageParityTest` (1), `ParameterCatalogContractTest` (4), `OpenApiConfigurationTest` (4), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `HandlerRolesSnapshotTest` (1), `CurrentUserFilterSupportTest` (1), `AnonymousRateLimitsTest` (29), `E5RouteParityTest` (2), `E5BackOfficeContractTest` (7), `E8ResponseContractTest` (11), and the whole unit suite (5171 tests: one failure in its first run, `S04ErrorContractTest`, fixed as below; that class then passed, 3); ITs `StepUpIT` (5), `BackOfficeSessionIT` (3), `StaffPasswordIT` (3), `CookieRefreshIT` (4), `IdentityCoreIT` (33), `IdentityContractIT` (11), `ConsoleProductHostIT` (11), `SecurityHardeningIT` (5), `ImpersonationHandoffIT` (14), `MongoRequestTimeoutIT` (2), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12), `SecurityInventoryIT` (4), `E2ContractIT` (8), `E3ContractIT` (17), `E4ContractIT` (65), `E5ContractIT` (41), `E6ContractIT` (31), `E7ContractIT` (29), `E8ContractIT` (76), `E10ContractIT` (44), `ListFieldsContractIT` (4), `ExportEngineIT` (12), `CensusIT` (73), `UniversalListsIT` (40), `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT` (22), `SignupSecurityFixesIT` (54), `ActivityIT` (36), `InstructorAggregatesIT` (10), `DashboardIT` (11), `NotificationLogIT` (6; its first run failed one case whose second, Spanish-locale token lacked `auth_time`: fixed as above), `TrainingIT` (33), `TrainingRegisterContractIT` (7), `AccountingExportIT` (4), `BillingFollowupsIT` (15), `InvoiceActionsIT` (14), `InvoiceConceptsIT` (4), `RemittancesIT` (18), `AuditQueriesIT` (13), `ConsoleProviderLockIT` (4), `PlatformConsoleIT` (43), `SupportAccessIT` (8), `MigrationReportsIT` (4), `ErasureIT` (21), `MemberDataExportIT` (2).
- Assumption: a "recent password confirmation" is a sign-in within the last 15 minutes, read from the access token's `auth_time`. The decision names no window: 15 minutes is the access token's and the sign-in link's lifetime, a technical constant like the 12 hours. The server checks when the session's sign-in happened, not its method: the app asks for the password, or sends a sign-in link to an account without one (`hasPassword = false`), and a link sign-in of an account that has a password also counts (whoever holds the mailbox can already set a password through «Recupera-la»).
- Assumption: re-entering the password is the existing password grant, which opens a new session with a new `auth_time`; no re-authentication route was added. The server does not end the session it replaces (the web does, OTHER-REPO), and that one ends at its 12 hours anyway.
- Assumption: "exports" are every personal-data export and file that S17 R-17-10 already closes to support as an export or a file: the S14 list exports, the export jobs and their download (`GET /exports` and `/exports/{id}` hand out the file's signed link), the member data packages (also the caller's own, `/me/data-export`, when the caller holds a staff role), the accounting export, the instructor's week PDF and the migration report. "Remittance file downloads" are `/remittances/{id}/file` (it issues the pain.008's five-minute signed link) and `/spreadsheet`; the signed file links carry no bearer, so issuing them is the guarded step. Creating or sending a remittance is not a file download and is not asked.
- Assumption: "bank-account changes" are a member's bank account changed by staff (`PATCH /members/{id}/payment-method`, and the pending signup's `paymentMethod` in `PATCH /members/{id}`); the club's creditor IBAN lives in its SEPA provider configuration and is covered by the payment-provider routes. A member's own payment method (member routes), the anonymous signup form and `club:apply` are not staff sessions and are not asked. "Payment-provider changes" are `PUT`/`DELETE /platform/clubs/{id}/payment-providers/{provider}`; the Stripe connection test changes nothing, and creating a club (`POST /platform/clubs`, its credentials are environment references) changes no existing provider.
- Assumption: "staff" is the token's ADMIN, INSTRUCTOR or AGILITYHUB_ADMIN authority (the decision's ADMIN, INSTRUCTOR, PLATFORM); an account with a staff role that exports its own member data is asked too. A support token is staff with its platform administrator's console sign-in time; an impersonation token is a member's and these routes refuse it already.
- Assumption: "back-office sessions" are the `clubs-admin` client's (a club's back office and the console), and the 12 hours count from the sign-in that opened the session: a password, a link, or a handoff from the member app. A handoff therefore opens a new 12-hour back-office session from a member-app session that may be older (R-01-13 keeps working), while the step-up actions still need a sign-in of the last 15 minutes because a handoff keeps `auth_time`. If the owner wants the 12 hours to count from the original sign-in across a handoff, `TokenService.sessionEnd` would read the family's `authTime` instead of its `createdAt`.
- Assumption: a staff account is one with ADMIN or INSTRUCTOR in any club membership that is not ERASED, or with AGILITYHUB_ADMIN; the minimum applies when the person sets or changes its password through `PUT /me/password`. Passwords that operators seed through `club:apply` or the demo seeder are not the staff setting a password and are unchanged; shorter existing passwords keep working (no forced change).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-06: a `clubs-admin` session ends 12 hours after the sign-in, link or handoff that opened it, however often it is refreshed; other apps keep `auth.sessionDays`. Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-05 and §6 `PUT /me/password`: a staff account's new password has 12 characters at least (or the parameter when higher), members keep `auth.passwordMinLength`; `400 PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT {minLength}`. Same Canvis line.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-18 (new): the recent sign-in (15 minutes, `auth_time`) a staff caller needs for the exports and their files, the remittance files, a member's bank account and the club's payment providers, `403 RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED {maxAgeMinutes}` after the route's own checks, and how the app signs in again; §3 JWT: the `auth_time` claim (kept by a refresh, a profile switch and a handoff; a support token carries its console session's); §11 T-01-26; Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_ERRORS.md 403 row and S01 row: `RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` (S01 R-01-18, `details.maxAgeMinutes`).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md `auth.sessionDays` (the back office also ends 12 hours after its sign-in) and `auth.passwordMinLength` (staff accounts need 12 at least): notes only, no parameter added.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/MATRIU_PERMISOS.md transversal rule 6 (new): the recent sign-in of S01 R-01-18 on top of the role, checked after it.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json: `POST /oauth2/token` description and `Set-Cookie` header (12 hours for `clubs-admin`); `PUT /me/password` description and `400 PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT {minLength}`; `RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` in the 403 of the 24 annotated operations and of `PATCH /members/{id}` (description too). docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entries «AUTH-01 (a)», «(b)» and «(c)».
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin (back office and console) and clubs (the member and instructor app). Adopt the API snapshot. On `403 RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` from any of the 25 operations above, open a «confirm it's you» dialog instead of an error, then repeat the call once: when `GET /me` says `account.hasPassword`, a password field submitted as `POST /oauth2/token` `grant_type=password`, `client_id` of the app, `username` = `account.email` (the new session replaces the cookie and the in-memory access token; `401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS` and `429 LOGIN_LOCKED` keep their usual messages), and after it end the session it replaces with `DELETE /api/v1/me/sessions/{sid of the previous access token}`; without a password, a «send me a sign-in link» button (`POST /api/v1/auth/magic-link {email, purpose: LOGIN, client_id}`) and a text telling the person to open it and come back. Texts, e.g. title ca «Confirma que ets tu» / es «Confirma que eres tú» / en «Confirm it's you», body ca «Per seguretat, torna a escriure la contrasenya per continuar.» / es «Por seguridad, vuelve a escribir la contraseña para continuar.» / en «For your security, enter your password again to continue.», and for an account without a password ca «T'enviarem un enllaç per entrar; obre'l i torna a fer l'acció.» / es «Te enviaremos un enlace para entrar; ábrelo y vuelve a hacer la acción.» / en «We'll send you a sign-in link; open it and try again.»; a focused, labelled dialog with keyboard and screen-reader access as the existing dialogs; a unit test for the retry. Affected screens: the exports and their downloads in every list, the member data package, the accounting export, the remittance file and spreadsheet, D10's payment method and the pending signup's edit, the console's payment providers (a support access refused there means signing in again in the console and starting a new access), and the instructor's week PDF in the app. Password forms (`apps/id` account, back office, first-password step): show the minimum from `PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT`'s `details.minLength` (12 for staff) in ca/es/en. The 12-hour end needs no new screen: a refresh answering `400 REFRESH_EXPIRED` already leads to the sign-in screen; check that the back office does so in the middle of a task.
- Review note (p2-api-05-access, 14 Nov 2026): the server accepts any sign-in of the last 15 minutes, not a password confirmation as such: an account that has a password also passes the step-up with a fresh sign-in link (`clubs-admin` accepts the magic-link grant), so whoever controls an administrator's mailbox passes it without the password and without the noise of a recovery (N-26, other sessions revoked). The decision reads «re-enter the password when the login is not recent». If the owner wants that literally, the token needs the sign-in method (e.g. an `amr` claim) and `RecentLogins` must refuse a link sign-in for an account with `hasPassword`; that is a further change, not made here.
- Review note (p2-api-05-access): the 12 hours count from the sign-in or handoff that opened the back-office session. An ADMIN who also has a member-app (`clubs-app`) session, which slides 30 days, can open a new 12-hour back-office session from it at any time through the handoff (R-01-13) without entering credentials; the step-up actions still need a sign-in of the last 15 minutes because a handoff keeps `auth_time`. If the cap must force a new sign-in, `TokenService.sessionEnd` has to count from the family's `authTime` (the alternative stated in the Assumption above).
- Review note (p2-api-05-access): INSTRUCTOR is staff for the step-up, so an instructor in the member app is asked to sign in again (password, or a sign-in link without one) for the week agenda PDF (`/instructor/week/export`) and any list export it may use once its sign-in is older than 15 minutes; a staff account exporting its own data (`/me/data-export`, including the job polling of `GET /exports/{id}` in the member app's DataExportCard) is asked too. Until the web ships the «Confirm it's you» dialog (OTHER-REPO above), staff see the `RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` message on those actions and must sign out and in by hand: deploy the web change with, or before, this API change. The 15 minutes and 12 hours are code constants, not parameters.

### AUTH-03 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. A link request is queued on the magic-link executor and mailed once (`MagicLinkService.java:85-100`, `:159-161`). A provider failure ends as a FAILED notification row and a log line (`SystemNotificationService.java:120-127`), with no retry and nothing shown to the member or staff, while the screen keeps R-01-04's neutral 202. The 10/h per address and 60/h per IP buckets are per process and reset at each deploy (`RateLimits.java:12`).
- To decide: (a) a retry for N-25, N-27 and the recovery mail: how many, after how long, and only for timeouts and provider 5xx, since a resend after an ambiguous timeout can mail the link twice; (b) a staff view of failed access e-mails (a D10 badge or a list; the rows already exist); (c) whether the activation screen asks a first-time member to choose a password (new copy; WEB-01 asks the same for the web).
- NOT-CODE: size the Brevo plan for launch day (about 190 first sign-ins on top of club notices) and watch its daily quota. In the 8 November section of `docs/DEPLOY.md` (:1725), add that rolling back to a release from before that date turns general delivery off, so LOGIN and access-resend links stop and only recovery mail goes out (with `EMAIL_RECOVERY_ENABLED`), while the screen still answers 202.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): one retry of the access / recovery e-mail on a provider timeout or 5xx; when a member's access e-mail finally failed, a mark on the member record tells staff (API field; back-office display).
- Retry: `SystemNotificationService.deliverTo` sends an access e-mail once more, at once and through the same transport, when the first answer is a provider timeout or 5xx (`retryable`: the senders' `… timeout` and `… HTTP 5xx` errors). Access e-mails (`access`) are N-25 (`LOGIN`, and `RESET` through `sendPasswordRecovery`, on its recovery transport) and N-27 (`ACCESS_RESEND` and `ADMIN_WELCOME`); N-25's `EMAIL_CHANGE` variant and every other system e-mail are sent once as before. A 4xx, a 429, a transport failure, a suppressed address, an allow-list refusal or disabled delivery are not retried. The row records both attempts (`NotificationRepository.finish(…, attempts)`, `deliveries[0].attempts = 2`) and its final outcome; a WARN log line names the code and the provider error, never the address.
- Mark: the settlement transaction of every access e-mail writes `accounts.accessEmailFailedAt` (the instant) when it finally failed, and unsets it when the provider accepted it (`NotificationAccounts.markAccessEmailFailure` → `AccountRepository.markAccessEmailFailure`, only while the account still has the address it was sent to). It follows the provider's answer to the call, also when its callback already moved the row (R-11-08). `AccountRepository.changeEmail` (AUTH-02) also unsets it. `CensusIdentityService.login` adds it to D10's login block: `GET /members/{id}` (ADMIN) → `login.accessEmailFailedAt` (`MemberLogin`, required, `date-time | null`). OpenAPI snapshot regenerated; `docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md` entry. No route, role, error code, event, notification, parameter or audit action added.
- Tests added: `AccessEmailRetryTest` (17 unit cases: a 503/500/timeout is retried once for N-25 and N-27 and its success clears the mark; two failures are FAILED with 2 attempts and set the mark; the recovery mail retries on its own transport; a 400, 429, transport failure, interruption, suppression or invalid response is sent once and sets the mark; a first success is sent once and clears it; N-26, N-25 `EMAIL_CHANGE` and N-02 are sent once and never touch the mark; a suppressed account sets it; the provider's answer decides the mark when the callback moved the row first; the retryable set). `LoginEmailChangeTest.AUTH_03_theLoginOfD10CarriesTheAccessEmailFailureMark`. ITs: `SystemNotificationServiceIT.AUTH_03_anAccessEmailIsRetriedOnceAndItsFinalFailureMarksTheAccount` (a 503 then success: SENT, 2 attempts, one mail, no mark; two timeouts: FAILED, 2 attempts, mark set; N-26 sent once, mark untouched; a mark for another address is ignored; a later recovery mail clears it) and `LoginEmailChangeIT.AUTH_03_theRecordShowsTheAccessEmailFailureMarkUntilTheAddressChanges` (the record shows `null`, then the instant; a confirmed login e-mail change clears it).
- Run and passing: unit `AccessEmailRetryTest` (17), `LoginEmailChangeTest` (11), `AccountEmailServiceSurvivorsTest`, `MessagingApplicationTest`, `EmailConfigurationTest`, `ErasureNoticesTest`, `MagicLinkServiceSurvivorsTest`, `MagicLinkServiceSurvivors3Test`, `MigrationWelcomeDeliveryTest`, `PasswordServiceSurvivorsTest`, `NotificationMatrixTest`, `ArchitectureTest`, `E2ResponseContractTest`; ITs `SystemNotificationServiceIT` (22), `LoginEmailChangeIT` (6), `E2ContractIT` (8), `DisabledEmailIdentityIT` (1), `WelcomeRateLimitIT` (1), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12).
- Assumption: "the access / recovery e-mail" is the set named in this entry's option (a): N-25 (`LOGIN`, `RESET`) and N-27 (`ACCESS_RESEND`, `ADMIN_WELCOME`). N-02 (the signup welcome and the migration welcome, whose S18 R-18-16 delivery is at-most-once on purpose), N-53 (an administrator invitation) and N-25's `EMAIL_CHANGE` confirmation are not retried and do not set the mark.
- Assumption: the retry runs at once, on the same thread (the magic-link executor, or the access-resend consumer), with no pause: a pause would hold the single-thread magic-link queue on launch day, and the decision names no delay. A timeout is ambiguous, so a member may get the same link twice (both links work; three live links per purpose, R-01-04).
- Assumption: the mark lives on the account (`accessEmailFailedAt`), because the access e-mail goes to the account's login address; D10 shows it in the member's login block, next to AUTH-02's suppression mark. A later access e-mail the provider accepts, or a change of the login address, clears it; a sign-in by password does not.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-04: one retry, at once, of N-25 (`LOGIN`, `RESET`) and N-27 on a provider timeout or 5xx; no retry on any other error; a final failure stores `accessEmailFailedAt`, cleared by a later accepted access e-mail or a login address change; the answer stays `202`. §3 `Account`: new field `accessEmailFailedAt`. Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S03-cens-abonats-gossos.md R-03-06b: D10 marks, with its date, that the member's last access or recovery e-mail failed after its retry (`login.accessEmailFailedAt` of `GET /members/{id}`); §2 D10 row «Adreça d'accés» carries that mark too. Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/MODEL_DADES_PLATAFORMA.md `Account` row: `accessEmailFailedAt`.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json `MemberLogin.accessEmailFailedAt` (and docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md).
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin. Adopt the API snapshot (`MemberLogin` gains `accessEmailFailedAt`, `date-time | null`). `CensusRecordPage` (D10), row «Adreça d'accés» of «Dades i pagament»: when `login.accessEmailFailedAt` is not null, show a warning mark next to the address, beside the «rebot»/«queixa» mark, with the date in the club's time zone, e.g. ca «L'últim correu d'accés no ha arribat ({data})», es «El último correo de acceso no ha llegado ({fecha})», en «The last sign-in e-mail failed ({date})», with the same tooltip/screen-reader pattern as the suppression mark; nothing when null. A unit test for the mark.
- Review note (p2-api-05-access, 14 Nov 2026): the mark and the retry do not cover the go-live welcome of migrated members (N-02 `MIGRATED`, `migration:welcome`, S18 R-18-16), which is at-most-once by design: a failed or uncertain welcome shows only as `REVIEW_REQUIRED` in the operator's command output (docs/MIGRATION_WELCOME.md), not on D10. The staff fallback is D10's «Envia la benvinguda» (N-27 `ADMIN_WELCOME`), which is retried and marked. Keep the command's output of the go-live run and resend from D10 for every `REVIEW_REQUIRED` row.

### AUTH-04 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. A locked ACTIVE account answers `429 LOGIN_LOCKED` even to the right password (`IdentityService.java:35-37`). Each run of `auth.lockoutMaxAttempts` failures locks for `auth.lockoutMinutes × 2^level`, up to 1440 minutes (`LoginLockout.java:10-17`), and `level` goes back to zero only on a password login or a redeemed link (`IdentityService.java:47`, `TokenService.java:87`). An unknown address keeps answering 401, so the 429 reveals that an account exists. S01 R-01-08 prescribes the per-account lock, the doubling to 24 h and the 429, and also asks for no e-mail enumeration.
- To decide (an R-01-08 change): (a) keep it; (b) lock per address plus IP instead of per account, or cap repeated cycles lower, or let `level` decay; (c) answer `401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS` while locked and tell the owner by e-mail (a new notification), which also ends the enumeration; (d) an admin "unlock" for staff accounts and an alert on `LOGIN_LOCKED` for them. Meanwhile the e-mail link stays the way in (R-01-08 never locks it).

### AUTH-05 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. `POST /api/v1/auth/handoff` needs a non-impersonated bearer whose source family is live (`AuthController.java:56-72`, `HandoffService.java:33-42`). The code opens a new family with its own refresh token, independent of the source (`HandoffService.java:66` → `TokenService.java:99`), as S01 R-01-13 describes. `PUT /me/password` asks for `current` only when a password exists (`PasswordService.java:49-51`), as R-01-05 prescribes, then revokes the other sessions. Caddy sets no CSP (`deploy/Caddyfile:11`, where E11-T03 left it to the web build), and the web has none.
- To decide: (a) bind a handoff session to its source family, so revoking either ends both (a new field on the refresh token), and/or require the refresh cookie to create a code; (b) require a RESET-link session or a recent login (`auth_time`) to set the first password of a passwordless account (an R-01-05 change: a member who signed in by link days ago would sign in again or use «Recupera-la» first); (c) the CSP: a header in Caddy or a `<meta>` in the build, enforced at once or report-only first.
- OTHER-REPO (once (c) is chosen): web build. A policy such as `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'sha256-…'` (the inline branding script of `apps/clubs/index.html`) `; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, plus the origins the apps really load (API host, Stripe, images), checked on staging in the three SPAs.

### AUTH-06 — PARTIAL
- A password change now also ends the account's sign-in links: `PasswordService.change` calls the new `MagicLinkTokenRepository.revokeUnused` in its transaction, next to `revokeOthers`. Every live unused link of the account (LOGIN, RESET, WELCOME, ACCESS_RESEND, RECOGNITION; any club) gets `usedAt = now`, the way `trim` already retires an old link, so a link mailed before the change answers `400 MAGIC_LINK_INVALID`. S01 R-01-05 amended (+ Canvis line). No OpenAPI or catalogue change.
- Tests: `PasswordServiceSurvivorsTest` (2 new cases: a change revokes the links, a refused change leaves them; the first fails without the fix) and `MagicLinkTokenRepositorySurvivorsTest` (1 new case on the filter and the update) ran and passed, with `ArchitectureTest`. New IT case `IdentityCoreIT.AUTH_06_aPasswordChangeEndsTheMagicLinksMailedBeforeIt` (an older LOGIN and ACCESS_RESEND link are refused, a later link works): not run (MongoDB).
- Not changed: the API has no "close other sessions" action (only `DELETE /me/sessions/{id}`, one session; whether a logout should also end pending links is a choice), and nothing blocks an account (`Account.Status.BLOCKED` has no writer; a link of an account that is not ACTIVE is refused at redemption, `TokenService.java:83` → `IdentityService.java:62`).
- To decide: redeem the link on a button press instead of on load (mail scanners that run scripts would no longer spend it; it adds a tap and new copy to the activation screen).
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs. `ActivationPage` (`apps/clubs/src/App.tsx:1107-1126`) should drop `t`/`token` from the address with `history.replaceState` before `exchangeMagicLink`, keeping `purpose`, as the handoff effect does at `:840-848`. `apps/clubs-admin` (`App.tsx:966`) and `apps/id` (`App.tsx:450-458`) already do.

### AUTH-08 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. The rules are length ≥ `auth.passwordMinLength` (8), not the e-mail, and the HIBP range lookup (`PasswordService.java:55-57`). The lookup accepts the password on a timeout or any error after a warning log (`CompromisedPasswords.java:45-52`). The three catalogue entries are `min: 0` and `editableBy: PLATFORM` (`catalog.yaml:43-56`, `:1096-1121`): `auth.passwordMinLength = 0` drops the length rule, `auth.lockoutMinutes = 0` switches the lock off, and `auth.lockoutMaxAttempts = 0` locks at the first failure (`LoginLockout.java:12-17`).
- To decide: (a) the minimum length (8, 10, or 12 for staff; a per-role minimum is a new parameter and an R-01-05 change); (b) floors for the three parameters, changed in `catalog.yaml` and `CATALEG_PARAMETRES` together (the same question as PRIV-02's retention floors); (c) when the breach lookup fails: keep accepting (today's reading of A2), retry once (up to 2 s more per change), or refuse with a "try again" error (a new error code); and whether to record a security event (a new type in the S14 catalogue) besides the log line.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): members keep a minimum of 8 characters; floors in the catalogue so that none of `auth.passwordMinLength`, `auth.lockoutMinutes` and `auth.lockoutMaxAttempts` can be set to 0 (minimum 8, 1 and 3); the breached-password lookup keeps accepting on failure (no change).
- Code: `catalog.yaml` `constraints.min` 8 (`auth.passwordMinLength`), 1 (`auth.lockoutMinutes`), 3 (`auth.lockoutMaxAttempts`), instead of 0; defaults (8, 15, 5) and `editableBy: PLATFORM` unchanged. As for PRIV-02's floors, the existing machinery applies them: `PUT /platform/clubs/{id}/parameters/{key}` (and a club definition applied with `club:apply`) under the floor → `400 PARAMETER_INVALID` (`ParameterValidator`); an override already stored under it is ignored by `ClubConfigService` (`ParameterInvalidOverride` warning), so the product default applies to the password check and the lockout; `GET /platform/clubs/{id}/parameters` and `GET /parameters` publish the new `constraints.min`. No Java change: `PasswordService` (members `auth.passwordMinLength`, staff 12 from AUTH-01), `LoginLockout` and `CompromisedPasswords` (still accepts the password when the HIBP lookup times out or fails, with its warning log) are untouched. No route, schema, error or OpenAPI change.
- Tests added: `ParameterCatalogContractTest.AUTH_08_theAccessFloorsOfTheDocumentAreTheCatalogMinimums` (each «**mínim N**» / «**mínims a · b**» in the last column of an `auth.*` row of CATALEG_PARAMETRES is the catalogue's `min`, exactly the three keys; the floor is accepted, one under it and 0 are refused `PARAMETER_INVALID`; `editableBy` stays PLATFORM; the members' default stays 8); `PlatformConsoleIT.AUTH_08_theConsoleKeepsThePasswordAndLockoutParametersAtOrAboveTheirFloors` (over HTTP: the console's parameter list publishes `constraints.min` 8/1/3, a `PUT` of 0 or of the floor minus one is `400 PARAMETER_INVALID`, the floor itself is `200`); `IdentityCoreIT.AUTH_08_overridesStoredUnderTheAccessFloorsAreIgnoredAndTheLockoutKeepsItsDefaults` (overrides of 0 written to Mongo for the three keys are ignored: the settings read 8/15/5 and the fifth wrong password locks for 900 s, where a stored 0 would have locked at the first or never). The PRIV-02 floors test reads the third-last column and is unaffected (its three retention floors stay the only ones it finds).
- Run and passing: unit `ParameterCatalogContractTest` (5), `ParameterValidatorTest` (9), `ClubConfigServiceTest` (5), `PasswordServiceSurvivorsTest` (9); ITs `PlatformConsoleIT` (44), `IdentityCoreIT` (34), `SettingsIT` (23).
- Assumption: the specs give no better floors (S01 R-01-08 and Annex A only state the defaults 5 attempts / 15 minutes, R-01-03 the default 8), so the decision's 8, 1 and 3 are taken as they are; no maximum is added (the decision asks for floors only). The floors are documented where each row keeps its notes: the notes column of `auth.passwordMinLength` (section table) and the «Spec» column of the Annex A lockout row, as `auth.refreshReuseGraceSeconds` already documents its range there.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md `auth.passwordMinLength` (**mínim 8**), `auth.lockoutMinutes` · `auth.lockoutMaxAttempts` (**mínims 1 · 3**) and a paragraph «Pisos de l'accés» (refused below, a stored override below is ignored).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-05 (the parameter never goes under 8) and R-01-08 (the lockout parameters' floors, at least 3 attempts and 1 minute); Canvis line of 13-11-2026.

### AUTH-09 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `POST /oauth2/token` (every grant, including the cookie refresh each app start makes) and `POST /api/v1/auth/magic-link` share `Route.TOKEN`, 30 per minute per remote address (`RateLimitFilter.java:130`, `application.yml:194`), held in per-process buckets (`RateLimits.java:12`). S01 R-01-08 prescribes «`/oauth2/token` 30/min» per IP.
- To decide (an R-01-08 change): give the refresh grant its own bucket and capacity, or count it per refresh family or account instead of per IP; and whether 30/min per address is enough for password and link grants behind a shared address (club Wi-Fi, carrier NAT). Shared counters only matter with more than one API process.
- Related: INC-80 W14 (back-office sidebar links are full page loads, one token rotation per click; `docs/INCIDENCIES_OBERTES.md:92`) is a web defect planned for after launch; fixing it removes most of the refresh traffic.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): the refresh grant gets its own rate-limit bucket, separate from the 30-per-minute per-IP bucket of the password and link grants (many members share the club's Wi-Fi address).
- Code: new `RateLimits.Route.TOKEN_REFRESH`. `RateLimitFilter` sends a `POST /oauth2/token` whose single `grant_type` is `refresh_token` (`refreshGrant`) to that bucket instead of `TOKEN`; every other grant (password, magic link, handoff, authorization code), a request with two `grant_type` values and `POST /api/v1/auth/magic-link` stay in `TOKEN` (30/min per address), and the per-username `TOKEN_ACCOUNT` bucket still applies only to them. The capacity is configuration, like the other buckets: `core.security.rate-limits.token-refresh` = 120 per minute per address (`application.yml`, `SecurityBaselineConfiguration.RateLimitConfiguration.tokenRefresh`, `docs/DEPLOY.md` table). A refusal is the same `429 RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After` and a `RATE_LIMITED` security event whose `details.limit` is `TOKEN_REFRESH`. The cookie refresh's own pre-check (an unreadable `ah_refresh` cookie answers before the rate limit) is unchanged. No route, error, parameter, event or OpenAPI change (the token endpoint's 429 description names no rate).
- Tests added: `AnonymousRateLimitsTest.AUTH_09_theRefreshGrantHasItsOwnBucketApartFromThePasswordAndLinkGrants` (exhausting the refresh bucket leaves the password grant and the magic-link route open, and the reverse; another address has its own refresh bucket) and `AUTH_09_onlyASingleRefreshGrantTypeTakesTheRefreshBucket` (two `grant_type` values and other grants count as token requests; a `grant_type` on the magic-link route does not move it); `RateLimitIT.AUTH_09_refreshGrantsHaveTheirOwnAddressQuotaApartFromPasswordAndLinkGrants` (over HTTP with the configured buckets: after 31 password grants and a refused magic link from one address, 120 refresh grants from it are not limited, the 121st is `429 RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After: 60` and a `RATE_LIMITED` event `{limit: TOKEN_REFRESH}`, and another address still refreshes).
- Run and passing: unit `AnonymousRateLimitsTest` (31), `RateLimitsTest` (5); ITs `RateLimitIT` (11), `SecurityHardeningIT` (5), `SecurityEventHashIT` (2).
- Assumption: the decision names no key or capacity for the new bucket. It keeps R-01-08's per-address key (a refresh token is single-use, so it cannot key a bucket, and counting per account would need a database lookup before the grant) and takes 120 per minute, the per-address capacity that every other anonymous OAuth route (`/oauth2/*`, discovery, authorize) already has, so that a club's Wi-Fi full of members starting the app is not refused; it can be changed by configuration (`CORE_SECURITY_RATELIMITS_TOKENREFRESH_CAPACITY`) without a release. The password and link grants keep 30 per minute per address, as the decision states. Counters stay in memory (one API process, ADR-003), as before.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md R-01-08: the `refresh_token` grant counts in its own per-IP quota of 120/min, apart from the 30/min of the password, magic-link and other grants and of `/auth/magic-link`; T-01-15 gains the case; Canvis line of 13-11-2026.
- Review note (review of p2-api-06-access-limits, 14 Nov 2026): accepted. The decision gave no capacity for the refresh bucket. The 120 refreshes per minute per address (the per-address capacity of the other anonymous OAuth routes) is the implementer's choice, yet S01 R-01-08 now states it as the rule. The owner should confirm or change the value. It is configuration (`CORE_SECURITY_RATELIMITS_TOKENREFRESH_CAPACITY`), so changing it needs no release. Reviewed: a request that names `refresh_token` cannot use this bucket to try passwords, because the server then runs only the refresh grant, and a request with two `grant_type` values stays in the 30/min bucket. Re-run and passed: `AnonymousRateLimitsTest` (33), `RateLimitsTest` (5), `RateLimitIT` (12), `SecurityHardeningIT` (5), `SecurityEventHashIT` (2).

### AUTH-10 — NEEDS-DECISION
- The same question as AUTHZ-07 (api-03-authz-tenancy); no code touched. The per-request check refuses only an account that is not ACTIVE or a token issued before `accessRevokedAt` (`AccountStateFilter.java:29-35`, `AccountService.java:65-69`); roles, club and member come from the JWT (`TokenService.java:272-286`). A logout or session revocation ends the refresh family only, and a suspension revokes the club's refresh tokens (`TeamMembershipService.java:78-80`); the access token in hand lasts its 15 minutes (`TokenService.java:31`), which S01 R-01-10 accepts.
- AUTHZ-07's options cover this finding: its option (c), a per-membership marker compared with the token's `iat`, would also make a suspension immediate; ending the access token at logout would need a check of `sid` against the revoked family on each request (one more read).

### AUTH-11 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. `ar-app` (public, code + PKCE) and `learn` (confidential, password grant, `accounts:write`) are always registered (`application.yml:177-190`), and a confidential client without a secret stops startup on staging/prod (`OidcClientConfiguration.java:33-36`). Discovery lists `accounts:write` and the password grant (`OAuthController.java:74-77`), and the two Learn federation handlers answer `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (`AccountAdministrationController.java:83-101`). This follows A30 (Learn prepared but not linked) and S01 R-01-11/R-01-12.
- To decide: (a) keep both clients registered as prepared; (b) register `learn` and `ar-app` only when configured (secret or redirect present) and trim the discovery lists to the live grants and scopes; (c) remove them from the production configuration until those products ship.
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): set `OIDC_LEARN_CLIENT_SECRET` in production to a long random value kept only in the private env, and confirm that `ar.agilitydoghub.com` (the `ar-app` redirect) is a host the platform controls.

## api-05-billing-sepa

### SEPA-05 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `CollectionDates.defaultDate` returns day `billing.sepa.collectionDayOfMonth` of the billed month as it is, and `earliest`/`tooSoon` count two Monday-to-Friday days after the generation day without ever checking the requested day itself (`CollectionDates.java:17-30`; the audit's `:161-174` does not exist). The run takes that default unless the body carries `collectionDate` (`BillingSimulationService.java:119-122`, `BillingRunService.java:99-105`), and D6 sends one only after the admin accepts a 422's `earliest` (web `BillingModals.tsx:112-121`). S12 R-12-12 and T-12-29b prescribe exactly this (day N of the billed month, two business days counted from the generation day), and ruling E145 left the TARGET holidays to the runbook (`docs/DEPLOY.md:1111-1114`).
- To decide (an R-12-12 change): (a) roll a date that is not a TARGET business day forward to the next one (weekends, 1 Jan, Good Friday, Easter Monday, 1 May, 25-26 Dec); this moves `paidAt` and departs from the members' "day 1" text (`signup.text.paymentDay`); (b) refuse such a date with a 422 that proposes the next business day (reuse `COLLECTION_DATE_TOO_SOON {requested, earliest}` or a new code in the closed catalogue); (c) keep it and let the bank shift the debit. Also: whether `earliest()` skips the TARGET closing days, whether the lead time is counted from the day the file is marked sent rather than generated, and a date field in D6's generate dialog (OTHER-REPO, web, once chosen).
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): with the club's day 1, the January 2027 run (1 Jan is a Friday and a TARGET holiday), May 2027 (Saturday and holiday) and August 2027 (Sunday) need `collectionDate` in `POST /billing/runs` (e.g. 2027-01-04, 2027-05-03, 2027-08-02), which only the API accepts today; amend the runbook line "Count TARGET holidays yourself" to say how.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): a collection date on a weekend or a TARGET holiday rolls forward to the next TARGET business day; the admin can choose the date when generating the remittance, validated as a business day and against the lead time already in the code.
- `TargetCalendar.onOrAfter` (new: the day itself or the next TARGET business day). `CollectionDates.defaultDate` returns `billing.sepa.collectionDayOfMonth` of the billed month moved by `onOrAfter` (into the next month when the month ends on closed days); `earliest` now counts its two business days with `TargetCalendar.plusBusinessDays`, so it is always a TARGET business day; new `businessDay`. The simulation's `kpis.collectionDate` and the run's default share `defaultDate`, so both move together.
- `BillingRunService.generate`: a SEPA run's `collectionDate` (the admin's or the default) that is not a TARGET business day → `400 VALIDATION_ERROR {field: collectionDate, fieldErrors: [{field: collectionDate, code: NOT_BUSINESS_DAY}], nextBusinessDay}` before anything is numbered or written; then the existing lead-time check (`422 COLLECTION_DATE_TOO_SOON {requested, earliest}`). `POST /billing/runs` already took `collectionDate`; its description, `BillingRunRequest.collectionDate` and `SimulationKpis.collectionDate` say so. No new route, error code or parameter.
- Tests added: unit `InvoiceArithmeticTest.SEPA_05_aDefaultDayOffTheTargetCalendarRollsForwardToTheNextBusinessDay` (January, May and August 2027 with day 1, Good Friday, 25-12, a month ending on a Saturday, a business day kept, `businessDay`) and `SEPA_05_theTwoBusinessDaysOfLeadTimeSkipTheTargetHolidays` (23-12 → 28-12, 30-12 → 04-01, Easter week); IT `BillingCycleIT.SEPA_05_aDefaultDateOffTheTargetCalendarRollsForwardAndAChosenDateMustBeABusinessDay` (the January-2027 run on 30-12-2026: simulation shows 2027-01-04; a chosen Saturday and 01-01 → 400 with `nextBusinessDay` 2027-01-04; 31-12 → 422 with `earliest` 2027-01-04; nothing written; the default run and its remittance ask for 2027-01-04).
- Changed tests (they asserted the old behaviour): `InvoiceArithmeticTest.T_12_29b_theCollectionDayIsADayOfTheBilledMonthAndZeroIsItsLastDay` expected 2026-02-28 (a Saturday) for day 28 of February 2026, now 2026-03-02; `BillingCycleIT.T_12_29b_theCollectionDayParameterTheExplicitDateAndTheTwoBusinessDays` expected 2027-02-28 (a Sunday), now 2027-03-01.
- Run and passing: unit `InvoiceArithmeticTest` (9), `TargetCalendarTest` (16), `BillingRunServiceSurvivorsTest`, `BillingRunServiceSurvivors3Test`, `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivorsTest`, `SepaRemittanceWriterTest` (25), `ParameterCatalogContractTest` (6), `E8ResponseContractTest` (11), `ErrorCatalogContractTest` (7), `OpenApiRequiredContractTest` (3); ITs `BillingCycleIT` (23), `BillingFollowupsIT` (15), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`).
- Assumption: «validated: business day» refuses a chosen date that is not a TARGET business day (400 with the next one) instead of moving it silently; only the default date rolls forward. The check is the existing `VALIDATION_ERROR` with a new `fieldErrors` code `NOT_BUSINESS_DAY` and `details.nextBusinessDay`, no new catalogue code.
- Assumption: «the bank's lead time already in the code» stays two business days counted from the generation day, now counted in TARGET business days (a run on 30-12-2026 can no longer ask for 01-01-2027); it is not counted from the day the file is marked sent.
- Assumption: a default that rolls past the end of the billed month (day 0 or 28 on closed days) goes into the next month, as «the next business day» says; `paidAt` (P5i) is the rolled date, as it was the requested date before.
- For the owner: the member-facing texts that promise «day 1» (`signup.text.paymentDay`, `signup.text.monthlyPaymentIntro`) were not changed; in January, May and August 2027 the debit is on the 4th, 3rd and 2nd.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S12-facturacio-i-pagaments.md R-12-12: default date rolled to the next TARGET business day, lead time in TARGET business days, a chosen date must be one (`400 VALIDATION_ERROR {field: collectionDate}` with `nextBusinessDay`), example of January 2027; T-12-29b: 28-02-2026 → 02-03, January 2027 → 04-01, the 400; §2 D6: the generate confirmation offers the collection date field; §6 `POST /billing/runs` responses; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md `billing.sepa.collectionDayOfMonth` row (spec cell): a resulting day that is not a TARGET business day moves to the next one. Runbook `docs/DEPLOY.md`: «Count TARGET holidays yourself» replaced by the TARGET rule and the dates of January, May and August 2027.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated): descriptions of `POST /billing/runs`, `BillingRunRequest.collectionDate`, `SimulationKpis.collectionDate`; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin D6 `GenerateModal` (`BillingModals.tsx`): when the month has SEPA receipts, a date field «Data de cobrament» / «Fecha de cobro» / «Collection date», prefilled with `simulation.kpis.collectionDate`, sent as `collectionDate` in `POST /api/v1/billing/runs {period, simulationId, collectionDate}` (another payload, another key, as today). Errors: `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `details.field = collectionDate` and `fieldErrors[0].code = NOT_BUSINESS_DAY` → «no és un dia hàbil bancari (TARGET)» with an offer to use `details.nextBusinessDay`; `422 COLLECTION_DATE_TOO_SOON` keeps today's offer of `details.earliest`. Label and error text in ca/es/en, keyboard and screen-reader access as the other fields of the dialog, a unit test of both errors. Regenerate the API types from the snapshot.
- Review note (p2-api-07-sepa): the TARGET lead time makes the January-2027 run date-sensitive. Generated on Thursday 31-12-2026, its default date 04-01-2027 is refused (`422 COLLECTION_DATE_TOO_SOON`, earliest 05-01: 01-01 is closed, then the weekend; before SEPA-05 an explicit 04-01 passed from that day, counted Monday to Friday). Generate that run on 30-12-2026 at the latest, or accept 05-01. The member texts that promise «day 1» are unchanged (see «For the owner» above). Reviewed: no new route, role or catalogue code; the 400 is raised before anything is numbered or written; BillingCycleIT (24), RemittancesIT (19), AccountingExportIT (5) and the unit tests re-run green.

### SEPA-06 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `RemittanceService.file` serves any remittance with a `fileKey`, a `ROLLED_BACK` one too, always named `remesa-{period}.xml` (`RemittanceService.java:52-61,96`), which is what the published description of `GET /remittances/{id}/file` says (`docs/openapi/openapi.json`: "also of a ROLLED_BACK one, whose file is kept"); the web mocks, a unit test and the e2e assert that name. R-12-14 keeps the file and reissues the same numbers after a rollback (rulings E87/E89, T-12-13), `EndToEndId = displayNumber` (R-12-12), and the `FITXER_NO_ENVIAT` rollback past the collection date is ruling E156 (`BillingRunService.java:192-199`). D6 shows the download whenever `fileAvailable` is true (web `RemittancesPage.tsx:372-390`).
- To decide (an S12 and contract change, in step with the web mocks and e2e): (a) a distinct download name per file, e.g. `remesa-{period}-{seq}.xml` or `{messageId}.xml`; (b) for a `ROLLED_BACK` remittance: keep the download but mark it "cancelled, do not upload", hide it, or refuse the link (the stored file stays for the record); (c) show `messageId`, `count` and `total` (already in the `Remittance` schema) in the "mark as sent" dialog so the treasurer can match the bank's receipt (web only); (d) whether a rollback burns its number block instead of reissuing it (reverses E87/E89).
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): after any rollback, delete the earlier download from the treasurer's computer, and match the `MsgId` and control sum with the bank's receipt before marking a remittance as sent.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): each generated remittance file gets a distinct download name, never reused after a rollback and a new generation; the file of a rolled-back remittance is marked cancelled («do not upload») in its name and in the back office.
- `RemittanceService.fileName(remittance, extension)`: `remesa-{period}-{seq}.{extension}`, `seq` being the last part of the remittance's `MsgId` (`{clubSlug}-{period}-{seq}`; `SepaRemittanceWriter` numbers it one more for each remittance of the month, rolled-back ones included, and `{clubId, messageId}` is unique), so a month generated again after a rollback gets `remesa-2026-09-2.xml`; a `ROLLED_BACK` remittance's name ends `-ANULLADA-NO-PUJAR` (`RemittanceService.CANCELLED`). It names the signed link (`GET /remittances/{id}/file` → `fileName`, and the S3 or local download's `Content-Disposition`), the local signed route (read at download time, so a link issued before a rollback downloads under the cancelled name), the link's `DATA_EXPORTED` entry (`details.file`) and the workbook (`GET /remittances/{id}/spreadsheet`, `.xlsx`). The file is still served for a rolled-back remittance and its content is unchanged. Descriptions of the three remittance routes updated.
- Tests added: unit `RemittanceServiceSurvivorsTest.SEPA_06_eachRemittanceFileIsNamedAfterItsMessageSequenceAndARolledBackOneIsNamedCancelled` (sequences 1, 2 and 12, a cut slug, both extensions, `GENERATED`/`SUBMITTED`/`ROLLED_BACK`, no `MsgId`); IT `RemittancesIT.SEPA_06_aMonthGeneratedAgainNeverReusesAFileNameAndTheRolledBackFileIsNamedCancelled` (`remesa-2026-09-1.xml` and `.xlsx`; after the rollback the link, the download and the workbook are `remesa-2026-09-1-ANULLADA-NO-PUJAR.*`; the new generation is `remesa-2026-09-2.xml`; the three names in the `DATA_EXPORTED` entries).
- Changed tests (they asserted the old name): `RemittancesIT.download` (helper: the link's `fileName` is now computed from the stored `MsgId` and status) and `T_12_11_…` (`Content-Disposition` `remesa-2026-09-1.xml`, was `remesa-2026-09.xml`); fixture `contracts/e8-billing-responses.json` (`RemittanceFile.fileName` example).
- Run and passing: unit `RemittanceServiceSurvivorsTest` (3), `RemittancesControllerSurvivorsTest` (3), `RemittanceSubmissionUndoTest` (5), `E8ResponseContractTest` (11); ITs `RemittancesIT` (19), `InvoiceConceptsIT` (4, the workbook route), `E8ContractIT` (76), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`).
- Assumption: the sequence of the `MsgId` is the distinct part (the audit's `remesa-2026-12-2.xml`), the `remesa-` prefix kept; the marker is Catalan and ASCII (`ANULLADA-NO-PUJAR`) like the prefix, so it survives every `Content-Disposition` and S3 presigned name; the back office shows it translated (OTHER-REPO).
- Assumption: the remittance's Excel workbook is a download of the same generated file, so it follows the same name and marker; the XML content itself is not changed (the decision marks the name).
- Not part of the decision and unchanged: the invoice numbers and `EndToEndId`s reissued after a rollback (R-12-14, E87/E89) and the `FITXER_NO_ENVIAT` rollback past the collection date (E156).

- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin D6 «Remeses» (`RemittancesPage.tsx`): for a row with `status = ROLLED_BACK`, a visible mark next to its download buttons «Anul·lada — no la pugis al banc» / «Anulada — no la subas al banco» / «Cancelled — do not upload it to the bank» (a `Badge` with the danger tone, and the download buttons' accessible names say the same, e.g. «Descarrega l'XML (anul·lada, no la pugis al banc)»), keyboard and screen-reader access as the other actions; the downloads keep using the `Content-Disposition` name; the xlsx fallback name `remesa-${period}.xlsx` becomes `remesa-${period}-${messageId sequence}.xlsx` (with `-ANULLADA-NO-PUJAR` when rolled back) or is dropped in favour of the header; the mocks and the e2e that assert `remesa-{period}.xml` expect `remesa-2026-09-1.xml`. ### SEPA-07 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. `SepaRemittanceWriter` stores the validated pain.008, with every debtor's name and full IBAN, unencrypted at `remittances/{clubId}/{period}/{messageId}-{remittanceId}.xml` in the export store (`SepaRemittanceWriter.java:111-112,161-166`); `BankAccountVault` seals only the database copies (`BankAccountVault.java:72-84`, `BILLING_BANK_KEY`). The files are deleted only after `rgpd.retentionYearsAfterLeave` (default 6 years, `PaymentsErasure.java:64-73`) and an erasure never rewrites them (`PaymentsErasureRepository.java:13-17`), as S14 R-14-15/R-14-16 prescribe. The download and the Excel workbook (`RemittanceService.workbook`) read the same file.
- To decide: (a) encrypt remittance files at rest (with `BILLING_BANK_KEY` or a key of their own) and decrypt on download and for the workbook; a lost key then loses years of files, and a key rotation must re-encrypt them; (b) delete the XML a fixed time after the remittance is `SUBMITTED` (e.g. after the 8-week CORE refund window) and keep only totals, `MsgId` and control sum, an R-14-16 change (the bank keeps its own copy); (c) keep them and rely on volume and backup encryption.
- NOT-CODE: check how the production store is configured (`FILE_STORAGE_MODE`: provider-side encryption on S3, or an encrypted volume for the filesystem store), encrypt the backups, and escrow `BILLING_BANK_KEY` apart from them.

### SEPA-09 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `InvoiceState.failed` keeps `paidAt` (`BillingDocuments.java:188`) and the accounting export's `collectionDate` is `$paidAt` (`AccountingLists.java:44`), so an invoice settled by P5i and later returned (R-12-17, `PAID → FAILED`) exports as `FAILED` with a collection date. Rows are selected by billed `period` only (`AccountingLists.java:19`). The series is the issue year (R-12-08, `InvoiceNumbering.java:15`), so the January 2027 run is numbered `2026-NNNN`, as `docs/DEPLOY.md:1108-1110` records (E145). R-12-26 leaves the final format to the accountant (S12 §13 item 9, A36).
- To decide with the accountant: (a) what a returned invoice shows: no collection date, the last collection's status and date (the export pipeline already looks it up), or an extra return-date column (`failedAt`); clearing `paidAt` itself would rewrite the invoice's history (R-12-10); (b) filters by issue date and by paid date besides the billed period (new fields of the `accounting` list); (c) keep the issue-year series or number by billed period (an R-12-08 change).
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): tell the bookkeeper both quirks before the first month-end.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): the accounting export gets two more columns, the status of the last collection and the return date (`failedAt`), without changing the existing ones; filters by date and the series numbering wait for the accountant.
- `AccountingLists.COLUMNS` ends with `lastCollectionStatus` (the `status` of the receipt's last collection attempt, from the `$lookup` the export already made for `paymentReference`: highest `attempt`, then latest `createdAt`) and `failedAt` (the receipt's own `failedAt`); both nullable. The sixteen original columns keep their order, keys and content (`collectionDate` is still `paidAt`). `ExportValues.accounting` prints the status in words from the new messages `billing.collection.status.{CREATED,SUBMITTED,SUCCEEDED,FAILED,REFUNDED}`; the headers are `export.column.accounting.lastCollectionStatus` / `.failedAt` (ca «Estat de l’últim cobrament», «Data de devolució»; es «Estado del último cobro», «Fecha de devolución»; en «Last collection status», «Return date»); `failedAt` is formatted like the other dates (club-local `dd/MM/yyyy`). CSV and xlsx, direct and queued exports alike. `GET /billing/exports`' description lists the two columns.
- Tests added: unit `AccountingListsSurvivorsTest.SEPA_09_theLastCollectionsStatusAndTheReturnDateAreAddedAfterTheOriginalColumns` (the exact column order, the two projections, nullable, in the default columns); IT `AccountingExportIT.SEPA_09_theExportAddsTheLastCollectionsStatusAndTheReturnDateAfterTheOriginalColumns` (a SEPA receipt settled on 01-09 and returned on 04-09 through `POST /invoices/{id}/failure`: «Impagat», collection date 01/09/2026 kept, «Fallit», 04/09/2026; a receipt in the remittance «Remesat»/«Pendent»/empty; a cash receipt «Pendent»/«Pendent»/empty; 18 headers, the first sixteen as before; the xlsx headers in English).
- Run and passing: unit `AccountingListsSurvivorsTest` (3), `MessageParityTest` (1), `ExportStorageTest` (5), `BillingControllerSurvivorsTest` (5); ITs `AccountingExportIT` (5), `InvoiceActionsIT` (14), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`). No existing test changed: the existing ones read columns 0–15 by position.
- Assumption: «status of the last collection» is the collection attempt's own status (`CREATED · SUBMITTED · SUCCEEDED · FAILED · REFUNDED`, shown in words), not the receipt's (already the `status` column); «return date (failedAt)» is the receipt's `failedAt`, which a card failure also sets, and which stays on a receipt paid afterwards (its history, R-12-10).
- Not done, as decided: filters by issue or paid date and the series numbering (R-12-08) wait for the accountant (A36, S12 §13 item 9).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S12-facturacio-i-pagaments.md R-12-26: the two columns after the existing ones, a returned receipt keeps its collection date, filters and series wait for the accountant; T-12-19: the returned receipt's row; Canvis.
