### SEPA-10 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. Any `billing.*` parameter, club, census, catalogue or concept change after the simulation answers `409 SIMULATION_STALE` (`BillingRunService.java:305-319`). `POST /invoice-concepts` refuses only a zero amount (`InvoiceConceptController.java:89`), and an `allMembers` concept brings into the month every active member with a payment method, pack members included (`InvoicingService.java:176-180`, `InvoicingRules.java:241-242`), as S12's section of 2026-11-04 describes ("tots els abonats actius del club"). `billing.cashPeriodMonths` has `min: 0` and no maximum (`catalog.yaml:641-653`); the rules read it as `max(1, min(12, n))` (`InvoicingRules.java:221,225`).
- To decide: (a) a D6 confirmation that shows the difference against the previous month's total and asks again above a threshold (web, plus the threshold as a new parameter in the closed catalogue); (b) an upper bound for a concept amount, and whether `allMembers` should leave out pack and single-class members; (c) the allowed values of `billing.cashPeriodMonths` (`max: 12`, or only the divisors of 12), changed in `catalog.yaml` and `CATALEG_PARAMETRES` together.
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): compare the simulation total with the previous month's before every confirmation.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): generating the monthly billing first shows the total and the difference with the previous month's total, and asks for a second explicit confirmation when the difference exceeds a threshold (catalogue parameter, default 20 %).
- New parameter `billing.totalDifferenceConfirmPercent` (`catalog.yaml`: int, default 20, 0–100, block billing, module `BILLING`, CLUB-editable). New `TotalComparison` (payments domain): a second confirmation when the difference, up or down, is more than that percentage of the previous total, or when there is no positive previous total; the signed percentage with one decimal.
- `BillingSimulationService.comparison`: each simulation stores `comparison {previousPeriod, previousTotal, thresholdPercent, secondConfirmationRequired}` (new `BillingSimulation.Comparison`; the service now reads `BillingRunRepository.live(period − 1)` and sums the run's `byProvider` totals, all providers, as the run recorded them; another currency → no previous total). Responses: new schema `SimulationComparison {previousPeriod, previousTotal?, difference?, differencePercent?, thresholdPercent, secondConfirmationRequired}` in `BillingSimulation` (`POST /billing/simulations`) and `PeriodSimulation` (`GET /billing/periods/{period}`, which D6's generate dialog reads), absent for a simulation stored before; `difference` and `differencePercent` are computed from the stored figures; its nullable `Money` references are published as `anyOf [$ref, null]` (`E8ContractConfiguration.NULLABLE_REFERENCES`). The simulation already goes stale when a `billing.*` parameter, the census or the catalogues change, so a changed threshold or a rolled-back previous month asks for a new simulation.
- Tests added: unit `TotalComparisonTest` (3: the 20 % boundary up and down by one cent, thresholds 0 and 100, no or zero previous total, the percentage); `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivorsTest.SEPA_10_theSimulationComparesItsTotalWithThePreviousMonthsRunAndAsksTwiceAboveTheThreshold` (no August run; 50,00 → 60,00 exactly 20 %; 49,99 → 60,00; a run in another currency); IT `BillingCycleIT.SEPA_10_theSimulationComparesItsTotalWithThePreviousMonthsRunAndSaysWhenToAskTwice` (September without a previous run asks twice; October against September's run 1.164,00: previous total, signed difference and percentage, asks twice at 20 %; D6's month shows the same comparison; at 100 % it does not; after September's rollback there is no previous total again).
- Changed tests: `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivorsTest` and `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivors2Test` (constructor gains the run repository; the test plan's club carries the parameter); fixture `contracts/e8-billing-responses.json` (`BillingPeriod.simulation.comparison` example, round-tripped by `E8ResponseContractTest`); fixture `parameters-canic.json` gains `billing.totalDifferenceConfirmPercent: 20` and also `billing.sepa.settlementBusinessDays: 5`, the SEPA-04 key it lacked — `ClubDefinitionsIT.T_02_12_canicMatchesBrandingAndParametersFixturesAndSecondApplyDoesNotWrite` failed at e9f6e4c for that reason and passes now.
- Run and passing: unit `TotalComparisonTest` (3), `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivorsTest` (3), `BillingSimulationServiceSurvivors2Test` (1), `ParameterCatalogContractTest` (6), `E8ResponseContractTest` (11), `OpenApiRequiredContractTest` (3), `NullableReferencesTest` (1), `OpenApiNullableEnumContractTest` (3), `BillingControllerSurvivorsTest` (5), `ArchitectureTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`; ITs `BillingCycleIT` (24), `E8ContractIT` (76), `E8PersistenceIT` (5), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`), `SettingsIT` (23), `E2ContractIT` (8), `BillingFollowupsIT` (15), `ClubDefinitionsIT` (21: 20 pass; `T_17_01_cliExportsYamlAndExitsWithoutOpeningTheConfiguredHttpPort` fails as in it-baseline.txt, environment: offline jar packaging).
- Assumption: «the previous month's total» is the total of the previous calendar month's live run (`GENERATED`, `CHARGING` or `COMPLETED`) over all its providers, as its `byProvider` recorded it at generation — the same scope as the simulation's `kpis.total` (remitted waiting receipts included); receipts cancelled or returned since are not subtracted. Without one (the first month in this system, a rolled-back or skipped month, another currency) the second confirmation is asked.
- Assumption: «exceeds» is strictly more than the percentage of the previous total, up or down; bounds 0–100 (0 = any difference asks), CLUB-editable like the other billing parameters.
- Assumption: the second confirmation is the dialog's, like the existing strong confirmation of R-12-11, which `POST /billing/runs` does not carry either: the API stores the figures and the flag with the simulation (one place for the threshold, the previous total and the rule) and the web asks; `POST /billing/runs` is unchanged and records no confirmation.
- For the owner: (1) the January-2027 run, the first one in this system, will always ask twice (no previous run). (2) The server does not refuse a run whose second confirmation was skipped by a client; a request flag with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` (as the rollback's typed word) would be a further decision.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S12-facturacio-i-pagaments.md R-12-07: the simulation stores `comparison` and the rule; R-12-11: D6 shows the total and the difference and asks the second confirmation; §2 D6 generate confirmation; §3 `BillingSimulation.comparison`; §6 `GET /billing/periods/{period}` and `POST /billing/simulations`; §9 the parameter; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md: new `billing.totalDifferenceConfirmPercent` (int, 20, Quotes i remesa, S12 R-12-11, 0–100).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated): schema `SimulationComparison`, `BillingSimulation.comparison`, `PeriodSimulation.comparison`, descriptions of `POST /billing/simulations`, `GET /billing/periods/{period}`, `POST /billing/runs`; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin D6 `GenerateModal` (`BillingModals.tsx`, reads `PeriodSimulation`): below «Es generaran {n} rebuts per un total de {import}», a line with `simulation.comparison`: «{difference} ({differencePercent} %) respecte de {previousPeriod} ({previousTotal})» (signed, club formats), or «No hi ha cap mes anterior per comparar» when `previousTotal` is null. When `comparison.secondConfirmationRequired` is true, or `comparison` is absent, pressing «Genera» opens a second, explicit step before `POST /api/v1/billing/runs` (unchanged request): e.g. «La diferència amb {mes anterior} és de {differencePercent} %, més del {thresholdPercent} %. Vols generar igualment?» with its own confirm button (not the same one, no default focus on it) and «Cancel·la»; without it, today's single confirmation. Texts in ca/es/en; keyboard and screen-reader access as the dialog's other steps (focus moves to the second step's heading); unit tests for both paths and the absent comparison. D11: labels `admin-settings:param.billing.totalDifferenceConfirmPercent.label` / `.help` in ca/es/en (block «Quotes, packs i remesa»). Regenerate the API types from the snapshot.
- Review note (p2-api-07-sepa): the second confirmation lives only in the web dialog (OTHER-REPO, not built yet): until D6 ships it, generating a month asks once, whatever the difference, and the API never refuses a run whose second confirmation a client skipped. `differencePercent` is rounded to one decimal while the flag uses the exact amounts, so a difference just above the threshold (49,99 → 60,00) shows «+20,0 %» with the second confirmation required: the dialog text should not read as «more than 20 %» from the rounded figure alone. The commit also adds the SEPA-04 key `billing.sepa.settlementBusinessDays` to the fixture `parameters-canic.json` (a test-only repair, needed for ClubDefinitionsIT T_02_12). Re-run green: E8ContractIT (76), InvoiceConceptsIT (4), OpenApiSnapshotTest (12; `bin/openapi-snapshot` reproduces the committed file byte for byte), ClubDefinitionsIT (20 of 21, T_17_01 as in it-baseline.txt).

## api-06-payments-stripe

### PAY-03 — NEEDS-DECISION
- Verified, no code touched. A test-mode completion writes the same `PAID` row, intent id and saved card as a live one (`CheckoutService.java:288-322`, `:273-279`); no session, payment or event row carries the mode (`UpfrontPayment.java:22-25`, `StripeEvent`); a mode change only asks for new secrets and drops `lastTestAt`/`accountId` (`PlatformProviders.java:56,62`). After the switch a test card stays usable (`CardPayments.java:66`) and its charge ends `FAILED` with `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (`StripeCalls.java:36-41`, `CardPayments.java:124-128`). Only `checkout_sessions.providerSessionId` keeps the `cs_test_` prefix, and the audited `CLUB_PAYMENT_PROVIDER_CHANGED` entry dates the switch.
- To decide: (a) store the mode (`livemode`) on each checkout session, up-front payment and saved card, with a mark in D10 and the exports (a model change); (b) refuse a test-to-live switch while a test session is `PENDING` or a member holds a card saved in test mode (`409 PROVIDER_IN_USE {count}` or a new code in the closed catalogue), or invalidate those cards on the switch (`MemberCardInvalidated`, N-35 to each member); (c) only the one-off clean-up below.
- NOT-CODE, before entering live keys: list the `checkout_sessions` with a `cs_test_` provider id, the `upfront_payments` and packs they settled, and the members whose card was saved before the switch; cancel or correct those rows with the people concerned and invalidate the cards (members re-enter them through card setup). Keep `STRIPE` disabled for public sign-up until live keys are in.

### PAY-04 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `POST /checkout-sessions` answers the open session only for the same `Idempotency-Key` (`sessions.openFor`, `CheckoutService.java:109-112`); with another key, any payable row still `CHECKOUT_PENDING` answers `409 INVALID_STATE` (`:120`), which `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT.java:307-308` asserts. The web drops `checkoutKey` on `?cs=cancel` and on the retry button (web `SignupPage.tsx:2806-2815`, `:3009-3016`), and Stripe's cancel URL does not expire the session, so card payment is refused until the session lapses after 24 h (P5 step h). S04 §2 sends the member to retry "from the e-mail", which also comes with a new key.
- To decide: (a) web only: keep `checkoutKey` after a cancel, so the same key replays the stored 201 and its still-open Stripe session (same browser only; the e-mail retry and another device still get 409); (b) API: when another key asks for rows held by the member's own open signup session, expire that session at the provider first and open a new one (a completion that wins the race takes E34's refund path; changes the IT above and S04 R-04-26); (c) API: answer the open session's URL again (the URL would have to be stored on the session). Either way, add an end-to-end case against the real API rather than the mock (web `SignupPage.test.tsx:1161-1182`).

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): an applicant who backs out of Stripe Checkout can retry card payment at once (web and API agree; no 24-hour lock).
- `CheckoutService.create` (the signup checkout of `POST /checkout-sessions`): new `closeHeldCheckouts`, run before the request's transaction. When the member has an open signup checkout (`PENDING`, not past `expiresAt`, with rows) holding a `CHECKOUT_PENDING` row of this request's scope, and the request is not the retry of that checkout's own key (`openFor`, review #3 of E5-T28), it asks the provider to expire that checkout (`PaymentProvider.expire`, Stripe's `checkout.sessions.expire`) outside any transaction. If the provider does not expire it (Stripe refuses a session that was completed meanwhile; an outage) the request answers `409 INVALID_STATE` as before, writes nothing and releases its key (as every error of this route). Then `prepare` expires those checkouts on our side (`finish EXPIRED` + `UpfrontPayments.checkout(…, false)`, the rows back to `DUE`, as the provider's own expiry does) and opens the new checkout. A row that still waits for a checkout this request did not expire (a concurrent request's new one) keeps the `409`. Stripe's later `checkout.session.expired` finds the session already `EXPIRED` and changes nothing. The route's description says so.
- Tests added: unit `CheckoutRetryAfterCancelTest` (5: the provider expiry runs before the request's transaction, then our side, then the new checkout; a provider refusal answers 409 and writes nothing; the same key retried never expires its own checkout; a lapsed checkout and one holding no row of this request are not sent to the provider; a row still held by a checkout this request did not close answers 409); IT `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT.PAY_04_anApplicantWhoBacksOutOfCheckoutPaysAgainAtOnceWithANewKey` (a second key gets a new checkout of the same rows at once, the first one expired at the provider double and here; a provider refusal → 409 and the open checkout keeps its rows; the new checkout settles the rows and saves the card).
- Changed tests (they asserted the old 409): `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT.T_04_22_T_04_23_aCheckoutWhoseAnswerWasLostIsAnsweredAgainWithTheSameSession` (its last step: another key now gets `201` with a new checkout and the old one is expired at the provider) and `T_04_22_aSessionPastItsExpiryThatNeverHeardFromTheProviderGivesItsRowsToTheNextCheckout` (the step before the expiry: another key now replaces the open checkout; the lapse is then that new checkout's, which is not sent to the provider, as before).
- Run and passing: unit `CheckoutRetryAfterCancelTest` (5), `CheckoutServiceSurvivorsTest` (27), `CheckoutServiceSurvivors3Test` (1), `CheckoutProviderFailureTest` (3), `S04ErrorContractTest` (3); ITs `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT` (23), `SignupGateFixesIT` (26), `SignupFollowUpFixesIT` (15), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`). `SignupIT` (35) and `SignupMinorFixesIT` (23) ran with one failure each, not from this change: `T_04_20_pendingPaymentAndDocumentEditsPreserveTheConsentLedger` and `R_04_19_T_04_20_paymentPatchKeepsTheHolderTaxIdIbanAndMandateDateUnlessChanged` answer `403 RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` to a `PATCH /members/{id}` with `paymentMethod` because their synthetic ADMIN tokens carry no `auth_time` (AUTH-01's step-up, before this package); every checkout case of both classes passed.
- Assumption: the API carries the decision (option (b) of the first pass): the web already sends a new `Idempotency-Key` after `?cs=cancel` and on its retry button, and the e-mail's link and another device are covered too. Expiring at the provider first and refusing when it fails — rather than expiring here first and refunding through E34 a completion that won the race — is the conservative reading: a Stripe session that was expired can take no payment, so the same rows are never paid twice through two open checkouts.
- Assumption: only the signup checkout (S04 R-04-26) changes, as the decision names the applicant. The S12 extensions of the same route (`bookingId`, and `upfrontPaymentIds`, e.g. a pack from the app, `PaymentCheckouts`) still answer `409 INVALID_STATE` for a row that waits for a checkout.
- For the owner: Stripe's `checkout.sessions.expire` is now called on a member's retry; include «cancel on Stripe's page, then pay again» in PAY-07's test-mode acceptance.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S04-alta-publica-i-validacio.md R-04-26: paying again after leaving Stripe (another key expires the open checkout at the provider and here and opens a new one; a provider that does not expire it → `409 INVALID_STATE`; the same key never closes its own checkout; a lapsed one is not sent to the provider); §6 `POST /checkout-sessions` 409; T-04-22; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated): description of `POST /checkout-sessions`; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs signup (`SignupPage.tsx`): no API-driven code change is needed; keep dropping `checkoutKey` after `?cs=cancel` and on the retry button, so the retry sends a new `Idempotency-Key` (`POST /api/v1/checkout-sessions {memberId, signupToken, successUrl, cancelUrl}` → `201 {checkoutUrl, checkoutSessionId}` with a new `checkoutSessionId`). A `409 INVALID_STATE` now means the open checkout could not be closed (paid meanwhile, or the provider down): keep the generic «try again later» message. The `cancelled` notice («pots reintentar-lo des del correu que t'hem enviat», ca/es/en) can offer the retry button at once. Replace the mock-only cancel-and-retry case (`SignupPage.test.tsx`, the second key differs) with an end-to-end case against the real API: cancel → retry → a new `checkoutSessionId` and `checkoutUrl`.
- Review note: reviewed 86f4819 against the decision (`CheckoutService.closeHeldCheckouts` and `prepare`, `StripePaymentProvider.expire`, the IT changes, R-04-26, §6, T-04-22 and the snapshot). It implements the decision and is money-safe: the open checkout is expired at Stripe before anything is written here, a Stripe refusal (completed meanwhile, outage) gives `409` and changes nothing, two concurrent retries share Stripe's `expire:{sessionId}` idempotency key and the second meets the first's new checkout under the census lock (`409`), and a checkout still being opened by a concurrent request is caught by the existing `answered` check (that request answers `409` and its Stripe session is expired). The log line carries only ids and an exception class. Build green; run again and passing: unit `CheckoutRetryAfterCancelTest` (5), `CheckoutServiceSurvivorsTest` (27), `CheckoutServiceSurvivors3Test` (1), `CheckoutProviderFailureTest` (3), `S04ErrorContractTest` (3); ITs `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT` (23), `SignupGateFixesIT` (26), `SignupFollowUpFixesIT` (15), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, the committed snapshot matches). For the owner: (1) the route serves ANON (signupToken), MEMBER and ADMIN alike, so any second request with another key now closes the open checkout, not only the applicant's own retry: an ADMIN opening a checkout from D2, or the applicant on a second device, while the applicant is still typing the card on Stripe's page ends that page (the payment there fails and the applicant must use the new link). Before, that second request got `409`. No money is at risk, but the D2 action should say so (or be hidden while a checkout is open); (2) a signup request also closes a member's `upfrontPaymentIds` checkout (S12, from the app) when that checkout holds one of the signup's own rows, since both live in `checkout_sessions` without `bookingId`; same safety, same effect.

### PAY-05 — FIXED
- Verified, nothing to change in this repository: `POST /upfront-payments/{id}/refund` exists (`UpfrontPaymentsController.java:69-83`, S12 R-12-20, `{amount?, reason}` + `Idempotency-Key`). The back-office calls only `POST /invoices/{id}/refund` (web `InvoiceDrawer.tsx:636`); `MemberBillingBlock` lists and records up-front payments (`:103`, `:171`) with no refund; a rejection with a collected payment says to refund it "des de Facturació" (web `admin-census.json:626`). S12 §2's D10 row lists only [Registra un pagament].
- To decide (web and S12 §2): (a) a refund action on a Stripe `PAID` row of D10's «Pagaments a l'acte» over the existing route (full or partial amount, reason, confirmation copy); (b) keep refunds in the Stripe dashboard (reconciled through `refund.*`/`charge.refunded`), point the warning text there and write the treasurer's procedure in the runbook.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): a refund action in the back office for a card payment (sign-up, pack or single class): full or partial, with a reason and a confirmation; ADMIN only; audited; the provider refund goes through the existing refund pipeline.
- No API code change was needed: `POST /upfront-payments/{id}/refund {amount?, reason}` (`UpfrontPaymentsController.refundUpfrontPayment`, `PaymentRefunds.upfront`) already refunds any `PAID` up-front payment collected by Stripe Checkout, whatever its concept (`ENTRY_FEE`, `FIRST_MONTH`, `PACK`, `SINGLE_CLASS`, …): `amount` absent = what is left, a part otherwise (`422 REFUND_EXCEEDS_PAID` above what is left, `422 CURRENCY_MISMATCH`), `reason` required (`@NotBlank`, ≤ 500), `Idempotency-Key`, ADMIN only and not while impersonating (`BillingController.ADMIN`, in `e8-routes.json` and `handler-roles.txt`); a `MANUAL` row → `422 PAYMENT_PROVIDER_NOT_ENABLED`; not `PAID` (a `REFUNDED` one too) → `409 INVALID_STATE`. It reserves a `REFUND_UPFRONT` operation with the admin as `actorId`, calls `PaymentProvider.refund` through the pipeline (retries, outage deferral) and the `refund.*`/`charge.refunded` webhook settles it: `refunds[] {amount, providerRef, at, reason, byAccountId}`, `REFUNDED` when all is refunded, `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` audit with the reason. `GET /upfront-payments?memberId=` (D10's list) already gives each row's `status`, `provider.type`, `amountPaid` and `refunds[]`, which is what the action needs. The work is the web's (OTHER-REPO) and the spec's.
- Tests added: IT `CardPaymentsIT.PAY_05_anAdminRefundsACardPaymentForAPackOrASingleClassInPartOrInFullWithAReason` (pins the contract the back office relies on: MEMBER and INSTRUCTOR → 403, a blank reason → 400 and no provider call; a pack paid by card refunded in part (40 of 120 €) with its reason, then the rest without an amount, more than is left → 422, a `REFUNDED` row → 409; a single class refunded in full; each settled refund keeps the reason and the admin on `refunds[]` and writes a `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` entry with the reason; the booking id stays; D10's list shows the two refunds and `REFUNDED`). A signup row was already covered by `T_12_17_refundMetadataTargetsSecondRowBeforeProviderResultAndKeepsAdminProvenance`.
- Run and passing: IT `CardPaymentsIT` (124).
- Assumption: «audited» is the existing `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` entry written when Stripe confirms the refund (entity the up-front payment, amount, refund id, the admin's reason; a failed refund writes its reversal), with the admin recorded on the refund (`refunds[].byAccountId`) and on the operation (`actorId`); no entry is written when the admin asks, exactly as for the invoice refund of D6. No new audit action.
- Assumption: the action belongs to D10's «Pagaments a l'acte» block, where the up-front payments are listed (S12 §2), for the rows with `provider.type = STRIPE` and `status = PAID`; the refund does not cancel the pack or the booking (unchanged pipeline).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S12-facturacio-i-pagaments.md §2 D10 («Pagaments a l'acte»: [Reemborsa] on a card `PAID` row, the dialog's amount, reason and confirmation, the refunds shown); R-12-20 (the back-office action: ADMIN only, card payments of a signup, pack or single class, full or partial with a reason, the same route and pipeline, no effect on packs or bookings); §6 route row; Canvis. No catalogue or OpenAPI change.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin D10 `MemberBillingBlock` («Pagaments a l'acte», reads `GET /api/v1/upfront-payments?memberId={id}`): on each row with `provider.type = STRIPE` and `status = PAID`, a [Reemborsa] / [Reembolsar] / [Refund] action for ADMIN (hidden for other roles and while impersonating), opening a dialog like D6's invoice refund (`InvoiceDrawer`): the amount (default and maximum: `amountPaid` − the sum of `refunds[].amount`; «tot» sends no `amount`), the reason (required, ≤ 500) and a confirmation step naming the member, the concept and the amount; then `POST /api/v1/upfront-payments/{id}/refund {amount?: {amountMinor, currency}, reason}` with a new `Idempotency-Key` → `202 {id, amount, providerRef}` («Reemborsament sol·licitat: es confirmarà quan Stripe el processi»), then reload the list. Errors: `422 REFUND_EXCEEDS_PAID` (more than is left, also with refunds still in flight), `409 INVALID_STATE` (no longer `PAID`), `422 PAYMENT_PROVIDER_NOT_ENABLED` (not a card payment, or Stripe off), `422 PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID` / `429 RATE_LIMITED`, `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` (reason). Show each row's `refunds[]` (date, amount, reason) and «Reemborsat» for `REFUNDED`. Texts in ca/es/en, keyboard and screen-reader access as D10's [Registra un pagament] dialog, a unit test (amount default, partial, reason required, the errors). Also change the rejection warning (`admin-census.json` «caldrà retornar-lo des de Facturació») to point at the member's «Pagaments a l'acte». No API types change.
- Review note: reviewed a991e89 (spec and IT only; the route, roles, idempotency, validation, errors and pipeline were checked in `UpfrontPaymentsController`, `PaymentRefunds` and `PaymentAudits`). The API side of the decision is in place and the IT pins the contract the back office will call; the action itself is the web's (OTHER-REPO), so the decision is fulfilled only when that ships. Run again: IT `CardPaymentsIT` (125: 124 pass; the PAY-05 case passes; the one failure is PAY-02's flaky `PAY_02_aProviderOutageSpendsNoAttemptAndTheEventIsProcessedOnceItPasses`, described under PAY-06, which uses the `upfrontPaymentIds` checkout this range did not change). For the owner, on «audited»: the `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` audit entry is written only when Stripe confirms the refund, by the webhook, so its actor is `WEBHOOK`, not the admin; the admin who asked is recorded on the refund (`refunds[].byAccountId`) and on the payment operation (`actorId`), and the entry carries the admin's reason. Nothing is written in the audit log when the admin presses [Reemborsa], and a refund Stripe never confirms leaves no audit entry. This is how D6's invoice refund already works. If the audit log must name the admin, or record the request itself, that is a further decision (an entry at request time, with an existing or new audit action).

### PAY-06 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched: `StripeWebhooks.handle` (`:91-131`) has no `charge.dispute.*` case, so a dispute is stored `IGNORED` (`default -> false`, `:129`), and nothing reads Stripe balance transactions, fees or payouts.
- To decide: what a dispute does (a flag or a new state on the collection or up-front payment; whether the pack or booking stays usable; what a lost dispute does to the invoice), how admins learn of it (an existing intervention notice or new event, notification and audit rows in the closed catalogues), and whether to build a periodic reconciliation against Stripe (payments, refunds, disputes, fees, payouts) and in what format.
- NOT-CODE for launch: subscribe the treasurer to Stripe's dispute and payout e-mails, write the manual dispute procedure in the runbook, and reconcile the Stripe balance report with the app by hand each month.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): a Stripe dispute (chargeback) flags the payment and notifies the admins (new notification row in the closed catalogue); no automatic effect on packs or bookings. The flag is visible in the back office.
- New `PaymentDisputes` (payments.application), called by `StripeWebhooks.handle` for `charge.dispute.created`, `charge.dispute.updated` and `charge.dispute.closed`, in the webhook's transaction. It finds the payment by the dispute's `payment_intent`: every up-front payment a Checkout settled with it (`UpfrontPaymentRepository.forIntent`: a signup checkout settles several rows), otherwise the invoice's `STRIPE` collection (`CollectionRepository.byProviderReference`). It stores `dispute {providerRef, status, reason, amount, openedAt, closedAt}` on them (new persistence record `PaymentDispute`; new last component of `UpfrontPayment` and `Collection`, whose previous canonical constructors delegate with `null`; written by new `dispute(...)` setters that touch no other field; `providerRef` because Spring Data would store an embedded `id` as `_id`). The first event of a dispute publishes `PaymentDisputed {disputeId, memberId, amount, reason, upfrontPaymentIds, invoiceId}` (new `BillingEvent.Kind`, aggregate `Member`); later events only update the flag (`openedAt` kept, `closedAt` set with `won`, `lost` or `warning_closed`), and an older event that arrives after the close never reopens it. A dispute of a PaymentIntent Core does not hold is stored `IGNORED` and logged (WARN, dispute id and club only). No payment, invoice, pack or booking state changes.
- N-56 «Disputa d'un pagament amb targeta»: `NotificationCatalog` (OPERATIONAL, ADMINS → APP+EMAIL, `OPEN_MEMBER`, `member_name`, `amount`, `reason`, module `BILLING`), `BillingNotificationFacts` (the facts of N-55: the member's name, the disputed amount, Stripe's reason), product seed `seed/message-templates.{ca,es,en}.json` and staff copy `notif.N-56.staff.{title,body}` in `messages_{ca,es,en}` (Stripe's common reasons put in words; any other one printed as Stripe gives it), the template club (`seeds/club-template-default.yaml`); an existing club such as the Cànic gets the template seeded on first use, as N-55 was.
- Back office: `UpfrontPayment.dispute` (`GET /upfront-payments`, D10's «Pagaments a l'acte») and `Collection.dispute` (`GET /invoices/{id}` `collections[]`, D6's drawer, and every invoice an action answers), new schema `PaymentDispute`, nullable references (`Collection` added to `E8ContractConfiguration.NULLABLE_REFERENCES`). No new route, role, error or audit action.
- Tests added: unit `PaymentDisputesTest` (4: every row of the Checkout flagged and one `PaymentDisputed` with its payload, nothing else written; later events update the flag, the same state changes nothing, the close sets `closedAt`, an older event never reopens it, no second event; an invoice's card collection flagged and the invoice's member named; an unknown PaymentIntent, a non-Stripe collection and a dispute without PaymentIntent change nothing), `BillingNotificationFactsDisputeTest` (N-56's facts for a Checkout and an invoice dispute; no notice for a member no longer in the directory); IT `CardPaymentsIT.PAY_06_aStripeDisputeFlagsTheCardPaymentAndTellsTheAdminsWithoutAnyOtherEffect` (a pack paid by card: `PROCESSED`, the flag, the row otherwise identical, only `PaymentDisputed` and `StripeWebhookReceived` emitted, N-56 to the admins with the member's name; under review then lost: D10's list shows `lost` and `closedAt` and the row still `PAID`, no second notice; an invoice charged by card: its collection flagged in the drawer, the invoice `PAID`; an unknown PaymentIntent: `IGNORED`, no event).
- Changed tests (the closed catalogues grew by one row; no behaviour asserted before changed): `NotificationSpecTest` (62 → 63 codes, N-56 last, `PaymentDisputed` → N-56), `MessageTemplateSeedTest` (52 → 53), `TemplateClubSeedTest` (52 → 53, N-56 listed), `TemplateClubCatalogsIT` (`MESSAGE_TEMPLATES` 52 → 53), `E8ResponseContractTest` (the N-56 fixture; `PaymentDisputed`'s fields, 34 → 35 events, aggregate `Member`, 26 → 27 main catalogue rows; 43 → 44 forms with `Collection#disputed`), `UpfrontPaymentsControllerSurvivorsTest` (the contract record's new component); fixtures `contracts/e8-events.json`, `e8-notifications.json`, `e8-billing-responses.json` (`dispute: null` on the existing collections and up-front payments, plus a disputed collection); snapshots `notifications/seed-{ca,es,en}.txt` (regenerated with `-Dsnapshots.update=true`: N-56's staff text, 53 codes and 22 staff texts).

Not caused by this change and left as it is (PAY-02's test, p2-api-04).

The mark keeps Stripe's status and reason as Stripe writes them, so the back office can show open, won or lost.

- For the owner: the club's Stripe webhook endpoint must also be subscribed to `charge.dispute.created`, `charge.dispute.updated` and `charge.dispute.closed` (runbook updated); include a test-mode dispute in PAY-07's acceptance. The periodic reconciliation with Stripe (fees, payouts) was not part of the decision and is not built.

- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin, after regenerating the API types from the snapshot: D10 `MemberBillingBlock` («Pagaments a l'acte»): on a row whose `dispute` is not null, a danger-tone badge «En disputa» / «En disputa» / «Disputed» while `dispute.closedAt` is null, «Disputa guanyada» / «Disputa ganada» / «Dispute won» for `status = won`, «Disputa perduda» / «Disputa perdida» / «Dispute lost» for `lost` (`warning_closed`: «Disputa tancada» / «Disputa cerrada» / «Dispute closed»), with the disputed `amount`, Stripe's `reason` and `openedAt` in its tooltip or detail text (and the same in the badge's accessible name); D6 `InvoiceDrawer`: the same badge on a collection with `dispute` in its attempts list. Read-only (no action: the dispute is answered in Stripe's dashboard; a short hint «Respon-la des del tauler de Stripe»). The notification centre renders N-56 like N-55 (staff text from the API) and its `OPEN_MEMBER` action opens `/abonats/{entityId}`. Mocks and fixtures add `dispute: null` to `UpfrontPayment` and `Collection`. Texts in ca/es/en; a unit test of the badge states.
- Review note: reviewed 92400b5 against the decision (`PaymentDisputes`, the webhook switch, the persistence fields and their writers, N-56 in the catalogue, facts, seeds and snapshots, `PaymentDisputed` in the event catalogue, the contract schemas and the OpenAPI snapshot, the spec copies). It implements the decision: a flag only, no state change on payments, invoices, packs or bookings, no new route, role, error or audit action; the new fields are written with targeted `$set` updates and every existing writer of `upfront_payments` and `collections` updates named fields (`UpfrontPaymentRepository.update`) or is insert-only (collections cannot be replaced, R-12-10), so a later state change never drops the flag; the WARN line carries only the dispute id and the club. Build green; run again and passing: unit `PaymentDisputesTest` (4), `BillingNotificationFactsDisputeTest` (1), `BillingNotificationFactsReturnsTest` (2), `NotificationSpecTest` (6), `MessageTemplateSeedTest` (5), `TemplateClubSeedTest` (5), `NotificationSeedSnapshotTest` (3), `E8ResponseContractTest` (11), `EventCatalogContractTest` (3), `TemplateValidatorTest` (10), `StripeWebhooksSurvivorsTest` (12), `NullableReferencesTest` (1), `OpenApiNullableEnumContractTest` (3), `OpenApiRequiredContractTest` (3), `UpfrontPaymentsControllerSurvivorsTest` (4), `MessageParityTest` (1), `ArchitectureTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`, `InvoiceImmutabilityRuleTest`; ITs `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12), `TemplateClubCatalogsIT` (3), `E8ContractIT` (76), `MessageTemplatesIT` (11), `InvoiceActionsIT` (14), `E7ContractIT` (29), `CardPaymentsIT` (125: the PAY-06 case passes; the PAY-02 failure below). `CardPaymentsIT.PAY_02_aProviderOutageSpendsNoAttemptAndTheEventIsProcessedOnceItPasses` failed here too with `409 INVALID_STATE`; confirmed as described above (its `signupRows(2).get(1)` picks among all the club's rows sorted by random id, sometimes the one it already paid, through the `upfrontPaymentIds` checkout this package does not touch): a test defect of PAY-02 (p2-api-04), to fix there. For the owner: (1) N-56 is not sent when the disputed payment's member has been erased (the notice needs the member's name and the directory leaves erased members out); the flag is still set. Card disputes can arrive up to about 120 days after the charge, longer than the SEPA window that holds an erasure back (PRIV-04), so Stripe's own dispute e-mail (pass 1's NOT-CODE) stays necessary; (2) statuses are kept in the order events are processed: only a closed dispute is protected from an older event, so a late `updated` can show an earlier open status (e.g. `needs_response` after `under_review`) until the next event; (3) no audit entry is written for a dispute; (4) manual step: subscribe each club's Stripe webhook endpoint to `charge.dispute.created`, `.updated` and `.closed` (DEPLOY.md), or nothing reaches Core.
- Test repair (found while running p2-api-11-messaging): N-56 grew the closed catalogue, but two contract tests still counted the old size and failed since 92400b5: `NotificationCatalogContractTest.WP_11_A_catalogMatchesTheDocumentRowByRowInBothDirections` (Annex A rows 19 → 20) and `MessageTemplateSeedChecksTest.E7_T03_theSeedFilesAsCommittedLoad` (seeded codes 52 → 53). Only the two counts changed; both pass.

### PAY-07 — NOT-CODE
- Before announcing card payments: a scripted acceptance in Stripe test mode on the production host (webhook endpoint with every R-12-21 event type; signup payment, cancel and retry, expiry, refund from the app and from the dashboard, a declined off-session charge, a PAY_TO_BOOK timeout with a late capture if `SINGLE_CLASS` is on, the PAY-08 provider expiry and the PAY-10 mode refusal), then one real low-value live payment and refund, with the results recorded in the runbook (`docs/DEPLOY.md:656-659` and `:1294-1296` still say acceptance against Stripe is unproven; `bin/e8-smoke` covers part of the test-mode run).

### PAY-08 — FIXED
- New `CheckoutService.abandonBooking`: a `PENDING` PAY_TO_BOOK session expires on Core's side as `expire` does and then, after the caller's commit and outside any transaction, at the provider (`expireAtProvider`, the helper a signup rejection already uses; a provider failure is only logged). `SingleClassCharges.abandon` calls it, so P7's timeout (`PaymentTimeoutsJob`) and a failed checkout opening (`BookingConfirmationService.openCheckout`) now expire the Stripe session too (the adapter skips a session whose Stripe id was never recorded, `StripePaymentProvider.java:147-152`), so the member can no longer pay for a released seat. A session already closed (late completion, provider expiry) is not sent to the provider; E34's late refund stays as the safety net. S15 R-15-17 amended (+ Canvis line).
- Tests: `CheckoutServiceSurvivorsTest` (4 new cases), `SingleClassChargesSurvivors3Test` (1 new), `BookingsAutoConfigurationSurvivors3Test` (the delegation is now `abandonBooking`), with `CheckoutServiceSurvivors3Test`, `CheckoutProviderFailureTest`, `PaymentTimeoutsJobSurvivorsTest`, `S04ErrorContractTest`, `S08MemberFlowContractTest` and `ArchitectureTest`: ran and passed. `SingleClassCheckoutIT` (the P7 case asserts the provider expiry, the late-completion case asserts none): not run (MongoDB).
- Not done, to decide: sending `expires_at`, which S04 R-04-26 lists (`+24 h`) and the adapter ignores (`StripePaymentProvider.java:69`). Stripe accepts 30 min to 24 h after its own creation time. A booking deadline (`bookings.paymentPendingMinutes`, default 30, `min: 0`) is computed before the call, so sent as is it would be refused for every PAY_TO_BOOK checkout; the signup's +24 h sits on the upper bound, so a server clock ahead of Stripe's would refuse every card signup; and a value taken from the call time breaks the replay of a lost answer (E5-T30 resends the stored request under the same idempotency key). Options: (a) a value derived only from the stored request (e.g. signup `expiresAt − 5 min`; booking `max(deadline, now + 31 min)`, bookings are not replayed); (b) raise the parameter's minimum to 30 (`catalog.yaml` and CATALEG_PARAMETRES together) and send booking deadlines with a margin; (c) rely on the provider expiry above plus E34. Check the chosen one against a Stripe test account (PAY-07).

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): send `expires_at` to Stripe Checkout so that the session ends with Core's deadline: the booking deadline when there is one, never below Stripe's 30-minute minimum, and S04's 24 hours otherwise.
- `StripePaymentProvider.createCheckoutSession` now sets `expires_at` (`SessionCreateParams.setExpiresAt`) from new `providerExpiry(request, clock)`; the old comment «Stripe defaults to 24h…» is gone. A PAY_TO_BOOK booking's checkout (its request's metadata names the `bookingId`, as `SingleClassCharges.open` builds it) is sent its deadline (`Request.expiresAt` = booking time + `bookings.paymentPendingMinutes`) clamped to `[now + 30 min + 5 min, now + 24 h − 5 min]`; it is asked for once, after the booking's commit (`BookingConfirmationService`), so a value read from the clock is safe. Every other checkout (the signup's, S04 R-04-26, payment or setup; S12's `POST /checkout-sessions` for up-front rows or a booking's rows; `POST /me/card-setup` and the admin's card-setup link) keeps S04's 24 hours: it is sent Core's own `expiresAt` minus 5 minutes, a value taken from the stored request alone, so a retry after a lost answer (E5-T30, which replays the stored request under the same idempotency key) sends Stripe the very same parameters. When Stripe expires that session, `checkout.session.expired` closes Core's side as before (R-12-21). Constants `STRIPE_MIN_EXPIRY` (30 min), `STRIPE_MAX_EXPIRY` (24 h), `EXPIRY_CLOCK_MARGIN` (5 min). No change to Core's own deadlines, P7, E34's late refund, routes, schemas or catalogues.
- Tests added (unit, `StripePaymentProviderTest`): `PAY_08_aSignupCheckoutEndsWithCoresTwentyFourHoursAndARetrySendsTheSameExpiry` (Core's expiry − 5 min, encoded as `expires_at`, identical on a retry three hours later, inside Stripe's window), `PAY_08_aBookingCheckoutEndsAtTheBookingDeadlineWithinStripesWindow` (a 90-minute deadline sent as is; deadlines of 0, 10, 29, 30 and 35 minutes sent as now + 35 min; 36 minutes as is; 48 hours as now + 24 h − 5 min); `T_12_31_setupAndExpandedCardUseTheClubAccount` now also asserts a setup session's `expires_at` (Core's expiry − 5 min). Each fails without the change (`getExpiresAt()` was null). Run and passing: `StripePaymentProviderTest` (12), `StripePaymentProviderSurvivorsTest` (5), `StripeCallsTest` (6), `PaymentProviderContractTest` (2), `ArchitectureTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`, `ClockRuleTest`. No IT exercises the Stripe adapter (the test profile runs `FakePaymentProvider`), so none was added; the end-to-end check against a Stripe test account belongs to PAY-07's acceptance.
- Assumption: Stripe counts its 30-minute and 24-hour bounds from its own creation time, so both bounds keep a 5-minute margin for the call's latency and a clock difference (the value pass 1 proposed as option (a)): a signup checkout closes at Stripe 5 minutes before Core's 24 hours, and a booking checkout with the default 30-minute deadline stays open at Stripe for 35 minutes, until P7 expires it at the provider when Core's deadline passes (S15 R-15-17, pass 1), with E34's refund as the safety net.
- Assumption: a booking deadline beyond Stripe's 24 hours (`bookings.paymentPendingMinutes` has no maximum) is sent as now + 24 h − 5 min rather than refused by Stripe; Stripe's expiry then cancels the booking through `checkout.session.expired`, as P7 would.
- Assumption: the «otherwise» branch is every checkout that is not a PAY_TO_BOOK booking's: S12's checkouts and card setups take S04's 24 hours too, as Core already gives them (`PaymentCheckouts`).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S04-alta-publica-i-validacio.md R-04-26: `expires_at` = Core's `expiresAt` (+24 h) minus a 5-minute clock margin, from the stored request (the retry sends it unchanged), and Stripe's expiry closes the session; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S08-reserves-de-classes-i-llista-espera.md R-08-18: the PAY_TO_BOOK Checkout's `expires_at` = the booking deadline, never below Stripe's 30 minutes nor above its 24 hours from the opening (5-minute margin), P7 expiring it when Core's deadline passes first; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md, Stripe providers: the runbook no longer says Stripe's default 24-hour expiry applies; it states the `expires_at` each Checkout session is sent (follow-up commit, same ID).
- No catalogue, OpenAPI or other-repository change.

### PAY-09 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched: `POST /me/card-setup` needs only the caller's own member and an enabled Stripe (`MyBillingController.java:92-108`, `PaymentCheckouts.java:25-73`); on `setup_intent.succeeded` a standalone setup calls `saveCard` (`CheckoutService.java:281-287`), which sets `paymentMethod.type = CARD` whatever the member paid with before and keeps only the mandate sequence (`BillingCensusAdapter.java:73-85`). S12 R-12-22 offers the route "des del bàner de 12" without saying who may use it; the web shows it only to card payers (web `InvoicesPage.tsx:302-307`).
- To decide (an S12 R-12-22 rule): (a) only a member whose current method is `CARD` may open a setup (otherwise `409 INVALID_STATE`; a SEPA or cash member changes through the admin's `card-setup-link`); (b) members may switch themselves and the admins are told (N-38 on `MemberPaymentMethodChanged` goes to the member only; a staff notice is a new catalogue row); (c) keep it and state it in S12.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): only a member whose current payment method is CARD may open a card setup through POST /me/card-setup (otherwise 409 INVALID_STATE); a SEPA or cash member changes through the admin's card-setup link; the member app hides the entry for them.
- New `PaymentCheckouts.createOwnCardSetup(memberId, success, cancel, answer)`, which `MyBillingController.myCardSetup` now calls: the same flow as before (`create` with `setup = true`, unchanged and still used by the admin's `POST /members/{id}/card-setup-link` and by `POST /checkout-sessions`), plus a guard inside the write's transaction, under the Idempotency-Key and census locks and before the key's earlier session is looked up or anything is written or asked of the provider: the member's stored method (`BillingCensusAccess.member(...).paymentMethod().type()`, the census's view, newly injected) must be `CARD`; a usable card or one marked `invalid` (the banner case) both pass; `SEPA_DD`, `MANUAL`, no method or no member → `409 INVALID_STATE` (no details). The route's `@ContractErrors` and description list `INVALID_STATE`. No new route, role, audit action, event or catalogue entry; the guards that ran before (role, own member 404, erased 409, BILLING, provider 422, redirects 400) keep their order.
- Tests added: unit `PaymentCheckoutsSurvivorsTest.PAY_09_aMemberWhoDoesNotPayByCardCannotOpenTheirOwnCardSetup` (SEPA, cash, no method, unknown member: 409 under the census lock, no session lookup or insert, no row pending, no provider call), `PAY_09_aCardPayerOpensTheirOwnCardSetupAlsoWithAnInvalidCard`, `PAY_09_theAdminsCardSetupLinkStillServesAMemberWhoDoesNotPayByCard` (the link never reads the method); `MyBillingControllerSurvivorsTest.PAY_09_theMembersOwnCardSetupGoesThroughTheCardPayerGuard`; IT `CardPaymentsIT.PAY_09_onlyACardPayerOpensTheirOwnCardSetupAndTheAdminsLinkMovesAnyOtherMember` (SEPA, cash and method-less members get 409 over HTTP with no checkout session and no provider call; the admin's link opens a setup for the cash member, its `setup_intent.succeeded` moves them to `CARD`, and then their own `POST /me/card-setup` answers 201). The 409 cases fail without the change (they answered 201). No existing test asserted the old behaviour: every earlier `/me/card-setup` success is a card member's, and `E8ContractIT` accepts 409 for its method-less fixture member.
- Run and passing: unit `PaymentCheckoutsSurvivorsTest` (21), `MyBillingControllerSurvivorsTest` (5), `MemberBillingControllerSurvivorsTest` (1), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `ArchitectureRulesTest` (23); ITs `CardPaymentsIT` (126), `E8ContractIT` (76), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`).
- Assumption: «current payment method» is the method stored on the member (the census's billing view), not a pending readmission's submitted one; an impersonating admin on `/me/card-setup` meets the same rule (the admin's link is the way).
- Assumption: the check runs on every request, also the same-key retry of a request whose answer was lost: a member moved off card in between gets 409 instead of the earlier session's URL (that session lapses with its 24 h).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S12-facturacio-i-pagaments.md R-12-22: `POST /me/card-setup` only for a member who pays by card (an invalid card included); `SEPA_DD`, `MANUAL` or no method → `409 INVALID_STATE` with nothing written, and they move to card through the admin's link; the member app shows the entry only to card payers; §6 route row: `409 INVALID_STATE` (only `/me/card-setup`); Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated): `myCardSetup` description and `INVALID_STATE` in its 409 list; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs (member app), `apps/clubs/src/billing/InvoicesPage.tsx`: show `CardFailureBanner` (the only entry to `POST /me/card-setup`) only when the member's current method from `GET /me` is `CARD`, i.e. `paymentMethod?.type === "CARD" && (failedCard !== undefined || paymentMethod.invalid === true)`; today a last invoice `FAILED` with a frozen `CARD` method shows it even after the member was moved to SEPA or cash. `POST /me/card-setup` (MEMBER, also the impersonation token; body `{successUrl, cancelUrl}`; `Idempotency-Key`): `201 {checkoutUrl}`, new `409 INVALID_STATE` for a non-card member (show the existing error toast if it ever arrives); regenerate the API types from the snapshot; a unit test that a SEPA or cash member with a failed card invoice sees no banner.

### PAY-10 — FIXED
- `StripeWebhookSignatures.authenticate` now compares an authentic event's `livemode` with the club's configured `mode`, with or without the hosted-demo flag: a test event reaching a `live` configuration (a test endpoint's `whsec_` stored with live keys) or a live event reaching a `test` one answers `401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID` with its `SecurityEvent` and stores nothing, as the hosted demo already did for live events, and logs one WARN with the club and mode. An event without a boolean `livemode` is checked as before. S12 R-12-21 amended (+ Canvis line). No OpenAPI or catalogue change (same 401).
- Tests: new `StripeWebhookModeTest` (3 cases; the two refusals fail without the fix), with `HostedDemoStripeWebhooksTest`, `StripeWebhookSignaturesTest`, `StripeWebhookSignaturesSurvivorsTest`, `StripeWebhooksSurvivorsTest` and `ArchitectureTest`: ran and passed. New IT case `CardPaymentsIT.PAY_10_aSignedEventOfTheOtherModeWritesOnlySecurityEvent`: not run (MongoDB).
- To decide (S17 R-17-05 changes): (a) refuse `mode: test` on the `prod` profile once live keys were saved, or only with an explicit override; (b) run the connection test (`ping`) as part of saving keys and refuse keys that fail it (today a separate, optional call); (c) refuse disabling Stripe or changing its keys or mode while a checkout session is `PENDING` and not past `expiresAt`, or a refund or charge is in flight, with `409 PROVIDER_IN_USE {count}` or a new code in the closed catalogue; (d) accept restricted `rk_` keys limited to what the adapter calls (Checkout sessions, PaymentIntents, refunds, customers, payment methods, account read).
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): a disabled provider makes the webhook answer 404 (`BillingContractAccess.java:159-165`), so disable Stripe or rotate its keys only when no checkout is open.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): on the prod profile, once live keys have been saved, switching to test mode needs an explicit override flag; saving keys runs the connection test and refuses keys that fail it.
- Connection test on save: new `PlatformProviders.testKey(name, input)` tests a STRIPE save's new `secretKey` with that key and its `mode` (new port method `ConsolePaymentAccess.ping(mode, secretKey)` → `ConsolePaymentAdapter` → `PaymentProvider.ping(mode, secretKey)`, a default on the port, implemented by `PaymentProviderRegistry`, `StripePaymentProvider` (the stored key's checks — mode, `sk_{mode}_` prefix, hosted demo's test mode — before any call, then `accounts.retrieveCurrent` with a client built from the new key; the existing `ping()` shares the account mapping) and `FakePaymentProvider` (new `refuseKey(...)`, cleared by `reset()`)). `PlatformClubsController.updatePlatformProvider` runs it before its write transaction (network I/O stays outside, as `test()` does) and passes the result to `PlatformProviders.update(name, input, tested)` (new third parameter); a key the provider refuses or cannot be reached with → `422 STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED`, nothing written or audited. The save records the passed test as `POST …/stripe/test` does (`lastTestAt`, `accountId`, shown as `accountIdMasked`). Club creation (`PlatformClubCreation`, keys from env references) passes `null` and the save runs the test itself, inside the creation's transaction (bounded by the provider's 30-second call timeout). A save without a new `secretKey` (publishable key, `enabled`, webhook secret only) asks the provider nothing and, as before, drops `lastTestAt` and `accountId`.
- Prod override: in `update`, on the `prod` profile (`Environment.acceptsProfiles("prod")`, newly injected), a STRIPE save with `mode: test` for a club whose stored configuration is `live` with a stored secret key needs `testModeOverride: true` (new optional `PlatformProviderRequest.testModeOverride`, constant `PlatformProviders.TEST_MODE_OVERRIDE`); otherwise `422 PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID {field: mode, reason: LIVE_KEYS_SAVED}`, checked under the providers lock before any write. Live to live, test to anything, a first configuration and every other profile need no override. The route's `@ContractErrors` adds `STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED` (already in the catalogue); no new route, role, error code or audit action (`CLUB_PAYMENT_PROVIDER_CHANGED` as before).
- Tests added: unit `PlatformProvidersStripeKeysTest` (6: the new key tested with itself and the passed test recorded; a refused or failed test → `STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED` with no write, also when the save runs the test itself; nothing tested without a new key or for an input `update` refuses on its own, and a publishable-key save asks nothing; on prod, live to test refused without the override (`false`, a string, absent) with its details and accepted with it; no override on staging, for live to live, from test or on a first configuration), `StripePaymentProviderTest.PAY_10_aKeyAboutToBeSavedIsTestedWithThatKeyAlone`; ITs `PlatformConsoleIT.PAY_10_savingStripeKeysRunsTheConnectionTestAndRefusesAKeyThatFailsIt` (a refused key: 422, club version, providers and audit unchanged, also when rotating a saved key; a good key: 200 with `lastTestAt` and `****5678`, one audit entry) and `PAY_10_onProdSavedLiveKeysSwitchToTestModeOnlyWithTheOverride` (the target bean's `Environment` swapped for a `prod` `MockEnvironment` and restored: live saved, test refused without or with a false override, the live keys kept, accepted with `true`; outside prod the switch needs none). These fail without the change.
- Changed tests: `E10ContractIT.T_17_05_R_17_05_providerReadsAndWritesProjectOnlyNonSecretSettings` asserted the old behaviour (a key save showed no account until `…/stripe/test` ran); it now expects the save to show the account the connection test reached (`****5678`), the decided behaviour. `HostedDemoProvidersTest` and `ConsoleProviderLockIT` call `update(..., null)` (signature only).
- Run and passing: unit `PlatformProvidersStripeKeysTest` (6), `StripePaymentProviderTest` (13), `StripePaymentProviderSurvivorsTest` (5), `HostedDemoProvidersTest` (1), `PaymentProviderRegistrySurvivorsTest` (5), `PaymentProviderSelectionTest` (1), `RecentLoginRoutesTest` (2), `E10ResponseContractTest` (8), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `ArchitectureRulesTest` (23); ITs `PlatformConsoleIT` (46; the first run failed on the new IT's own assertion — the fixture club already holds a STRIPE entry — corrected to compare the stored providers), `E10ContractIT` (44), `ConsoleProviderLockIT` (4), `StepUpIT` (5), `SecurityInventoryIT` (4), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12, after `bin/openapi-snapshot`).
- Assumption: «once live keys have been saved» reads the club's stored Stripe configuration: its `mode` is `live` with a stored secret key. Removing the provider (possible only with no card members, R-17-05) and configuring it again starts from nothing stored; no history of earlier live keys is kept.
- Assumption: the «explicit override flag» is a field of the save request (`testModeOverride: true`), not a deployment setting; it is needed only for the live-to-test switch on `prod` and ignored otherwise. The refusal reuses `PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID` (the code the route already gives a mode the club may not use, as in the hosted demo) with `details {field: mode, reason: LIVE_KEYS_SAVED}` so the console can tell it apart.
- Assumption: «saving keys» is a save that stores a new `secretKey` (a mode switch always does): the connection test checks the API key; a webhook signing secret or a publishable key cannot be checked by a call to Stripe and is saved as before. A passed test is recorded with the save, as the connection test records it.
- Not part of the decision, not built: refusing to disable or re-key Stripe while a checkout, charge or refund is in flight (pass 1's option (c); the NOT-CODE caution above stays) and restricted `rk_` keys (option (d)).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S17-consola-de-clubs.md R-17-05: saving a `secretKey` runs the connection test with the new key (outside the transaction) and refuses it with `422 STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED`, nothing saved, a passed test recorded; on `prod`, live keys switch to `mode: test` only with `testModeOverride: true` (else `422 PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID {field: mode, reason: LIVE_KEYS_SAVED}`); §6 route row: `testModeOverride?` and `422 STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED`; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json (regenerated): `PlatformProviderRequest.testModeOverride`, `updatePlatformProvider` description and `STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED` in its 422 list; docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md entry; docs/DEPLOY.md, Stripe providers: the test on save and the prod override.
- OTHER-REPO: web clubs-admin console, `apps/clubs-admin/src/console/tabs/PaymentsTab.tsx` (after regenerating the API types): `PUT /platform/clubs/{id}/payment-providers/STRIPE` (AGILITYHUB_ADMIN, recent login) can now answer `422 STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED` when a new secret key is saved — show the existing «connection failed» message on the form and keep the typed values; a successful save already returns `lastTestAt` and `accountIdMasked`, so the form shows the account as after «Prova la connexió». When the API answers `422 PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID` with `details.reason = LIVE_KEYS_SAVED` (a prod club with live keys switched to test), show a danger confirmation («Aquest club té claus live desades. Passar a mode test fa que els pagaments siguin de prova.» / «Este club tiene claves live guardadas. Pasar a modo test hace que los pagos sean de prueba.» / «This club has live keys saved. Switching to test mode makes payments test payments.») and, on explicit confirmation, resend the same body with `testModeOverride: true`. Texts in ca/es/en, keyboard and screen-reader access as the neighbouring confirmations; a unit test of the confirmation and the resend.
- Review note (p2-api-10-payments-guards, 14 Nov 2026): verified and kept. Two points for the owner. (1) The «explicit override flag» is a field of the console's save request (`testModeOverride: true`), so the guard stops a mistaken switch but any platform admin can confirm it in the same request; a deployment-level flag (an environment variable an operator must set on the prod host) would be stricter. Say so if that was the intent. (2) Creating a club through `POST /platform/clubs` with Stripe keys given as `env` references now also runs the connection test, and it runs inside the creation's Mongo transaction (bounded by the provider's 30-second timeout) rather than before it as on the `PUT`; a refused key makes the creation answer `422 STRIPE_CONNECTION_FAILED`, which that route's documented errors (S17 §6, OpenAPI) do not list (they also omit the `422 PROVIDER_CONFIG_INVALID` this path already gave for a missing variable). The usual flow (create, then save keys in the Payments tab) is unaffected.

## api-07-privacy-data

### PRIV-04 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. Eligibility blocks only `PENDING`/`COLLECTING`/`FAILED` invoices and open up-front payments (`PaymentsErasureRepository.java:24-29`, `ErasureEligibility.java:22-29`), and `executeAt = max(now, leaveDate + rgpd.erasureMinDaysAfterLeave)` (default 30, `min: 0`; `ErasurePolicy.java:22-28`, `catalog.yaml:1516-1528`). At execution the member's invoices lose `memberSnapshot.fullName`/`taxId` and `paymentMethod.holderName`/`maskedAccount` (`PaymentsErasureRepository.java:32-36`), and the member's payment method keeps only `type`, `mandateRef` and `mandateSignedAt` (`CensusErasureRepository.java:64-78`). S14 R-14-14 and R-14-15 prescribe all of this. A later bank return can still be recorded on such an invoice (`InvoiceActions.markFailed`, `:83-99`, has no erasure check), but the debt then names no one.
- To decide (legal/accounting, an S14 change): (a) block erasure while a SEPA collection of the member is inside the refund window (8 weeks after the collection date; 13 months for an unauthorised debit), as a new reason of `422 ERASURE_BLOCKED` or as a later `executeAt` for SEPA payers; (b) keep the invoice recipient (name, tax id) and the mandate evidence (debtor, sealed IBAN) in a restricted "blocked" form until `rgpd.retentionYearsAfterLeave`, pseudonymising them only in the retention sweep (R-14-16 (a); GDPR art. 17(3)(b), LOPDGDD art. 32); (c) keep R-14-15 as it is and rely on the bank's and the accountant's own records.
- Meanwhile (NOT-CODE): until this is decided, cancel any scheduled erasure of a former SEPA payer whose last collection is less than 8 weeks old, and request it again later.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): an erasure is not executed while a SEPA collection of that member is inside the 8-week refund window; it waits (stays pending with the reason visible) and runs when the window closes.
- New block reason `SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW` (`ErasureBlockReason`, with `waits()`), reported by the payments check (`PaymentsErasure.blockReasons`): a `SEPA_XML` collection of an invoice the member pays (`invoices.memberId`, as `INVOICES_OPEN` reads it), in a remittance that is not `ROLLED_BACK`, whose `requestedCollectionDate` is on or after today (club-local) minus 8 weeks (`PaymentsErasureRepository.latestSepaCollectionDate`). The window is the new domain rule `payments.domain.SepaRefundWindow` (8 weeks; open through the collection date + 8 weeks, closed from the next day).
- Request (`ErasureService.requestMember`, `requestAccount`): that reason refuses nothing. The other reasons still refuse with `422 ERASURE_BLOCKED`, whose `reasons` now lists only the refusing ones. A request with the waiting reason is created `BLOCKED` with `blockReasons: [SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW]` (shown by `GET /members/{id}/erasure`, D10 and the console list; cancellable like any `BLOCKED` one), and `executeAt` is the later of the R-14-14 margin and the start (club-local) of the first day the last window is closed (`ErasureCheck.waitsUntil`, new default method; `ErasureEligibility.waitsUntil` takes the latest over the contexts; for an account request, over its clubs). `AccountErasureRequested` and N-52 carry that date. The daily `erasures` run does not take it before then, and executes it on that day after checking again.
- Execution (`ErasureExecutor`, unchanged): R-14-15 already re-runs every check, so a request that meets a SEPA collection inside its window when it comes due (a debit collected after the request) goes `BLOCKED [SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW]` and the daily retry erases it the first day the window is closed. A retention request (R-14-16) is scheduled as before and blocked the same way by the executor.
- OpenAPI: the descriptions of `POST /members/{id}/erasure` and `POST /accounts/{id}/erasure` (snapshot regenerated; `docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md`). No route, schema, error code, event, notification, parameter or audit action added.
- Tests: new `SepaRefundWindowTest` (boundaries: the last day inside, the day it closes, a leap year); `ErasureEligibilityTest` +1 (the latest `waitsUntil` over the contexts, an erased member not asked, only `SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW` waits); `ErasureServiceTest` +4 (a waiting reason alone gives `BLOCKED` with the reason and `executeAt` = the window's close, or the margin when later, and the event carries it; with `INVOICES_OPEN` it is still `422` listing only `INVOICES_OPEN`; a retention request stays `SCHEDULED`; an account request waits for the latest window of its clubs); `ErasureIT` +2 against MongoDB and the job runner (a collection of 27-08 → `201 BLOCKED [SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW]`, `executeAt` 2026-10-22T22:00:00Z, untouched by the runs of 30-09 and 22-10, erased by the run of 23-10, while a rolled-back remittance, a card collection, another member's collection and one older than 8 weeks would each have given another date; a debit of 20-09 collected after the request keeps the execution `BLOCKED` on 30-09 and 15-11 and it is erased on 16-11). Without the change the request would be `SCHEDULED` and erased on 30-09. Run and passing: `SepaRefundWindowTest`, `ErasureEligibilityTest` (5), `ErasureServiceTest` (12), `ErasureExecutorTest` (9), `ArchitectureTest`, `ClockRuleTest`; ITs `ErasureIT` (23), `RetentionSweepIT` (6), `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (12). The first commit's `ErasureIT` fixture inserted several invoices without `series`/`number` and remittances without `messageId`, which the unique indexes `invoice_club_series_number` and `remittance_club_message` refuse, so the first new case errored before reaching the rule (the run's result was read too early and logged as passing); a follow-up commit gives them unique values, and `ErasureIT` then passed 23 of 23.
- Assumption: «a SEPA collection of that member» is an attempt in a remittance file that may have reached the bank (`GENERATED` or `SUBMITTED`; a `ROLLED_BACK` one debited no one), of an invoice the member pays, whatever the attempt's later status: a debit later returned or refunded still holds the request until 8 weeks after its collection date (the simple, conservative reading). The 13-month window for an unauthorised debit is not applied (the decision names 8 weeks).
- Assumption: the 8 weeks are the SEPA Core rulebook's, the same for every club, so they are a domain constant (`SepaRefundWindow.LENGTH`), not a club parameter (AGENTS rule 3 covers club-specific values). The window is open through the collection date + 8 weeks and closed from the next day, at 00:00 in the club's time zone.
- Assumption: «stays pending with the reason visible» is the existing `BLOCKED` state with `blockReasons` (visible on D10 and in the console, cancellable, retried by the daily run), entered at creation for this reason only; `executeAt` moves to the day the window closes so that N-52's date («your data will be erased on …») and D10's «planned for» date are the real ones.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S14-tauler-auditoria-exportacions-rgpd.md R-14-14: a SEPA collection inside its 8-week refund window does not refuse the request; it is created `BLOCKED [SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW]`, `executeAt` = the later of the margin and the first day with the window closed, and runs then (R-14-15 checks again); the `422` lists only refusing reasons; example column; §3 `blockReasons` gains `SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW`; §5 state diagram and transition table gain `[*]` → BLOCKED; T-14-18; Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json: descriptions of `POST /members/{id}/erasure` and `POST /accounts/{id}/erasure`.
- OTHER-REPO: web apps/clubs-admin D10 (`src/census/MemberPrivacyCard.tsx` `erasureBlockLabel`, `packages/i18n` `admin-census.member.privacy.erasure.blockReasons`): a label for the new block reason `SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW` in ca/es/en, for instance «Un cobrament SEPA encara es pot retornar (8 setmanes): la supressió s'executarà quan es tanqui el termini.»; until then it shows the generic «Erasure is blocked.». A `201` from `POST /api/v1/members/{id}/erasure` can now be `status: BLOCKED` with `blockReasons: ["SEPA_REFUND_WINDOW"]` (the card already renders a live `BLOCKED` request with its reasons and its `executeAt`). No route, field or error code changes.

### PRIV-05 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. The planner writes `consents.privacyPolicy = {version: LEGACY, acceptedAt: <join date>, source: MIGRATED}` (`PlayoffPlanner.java:294-295`). `ConsentGrant.legacyPrivacy` (`:15-18`) reads any `{acceptedAt, version}` without a boolean as granted. As a result the consent register (`CensusConsents.history`, `:31-36`) reports a PRIVACY_POLICY acceptance at the join date, and `SignupService.appendConsents` (`:267-271`, via `ConsentLedgers.entries`, `:20`) persists it as a `granted: true` ledger row. Onboarding still asks for the current version. `GET /me/onboarding` returns the stored image permission (`MemberOnboardingService.java:19-26`). The web uses it as the box's initial state (`onboarding.tsx:132`) and sends it only when changed (`:176`). Admin and member edits both write `source: PROFILE` (`ConsentLedgers.java:33`, `MemberService.java:113-118`); only `byAccountId` tells them apart. No code in this repository bulk-sets image rights, so the ~1,066 granted values come from a data operation.
- To decide (club and legal): (1) the bulk-set image rights: reset them to "not recorded" (a data fix with an audit note), or keep only those backed by a signed form; (2) onboarding: show the box unticked unless the latest IMAGE_USE entry was written by the member's own account (or by a source the club accepts, such as an admin recording a signed form), and write the member's choice on every save (API: return `imageConsent` only for such entries, or add its source; web: always send it); (3) the migrated privacy marker: report it as not granted (`legacyPrivacy` false for `source: MIGRATED`) or as a third state, "no acceptance recorded in this system" (new copy in D10 and the data package).

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): keep the stored image-rights consent values; in onboarding the image-rights box is shown unticked unless the member's own account ticked it before (a value set by the migration or by staff does not pre-tick it).
- `MemberOnboardingService.imageConsent` (the box's initial state, `GET`/`PUT /me/onboarding` `imageConsent`) now returns `true` only when the image permission in force (the `imageRights` projection: the legacy object, or the latest `IMAGE_USE` row of the S04 ledger) is a grant written by the member's own account: `byAccountId` (the member's onboarding; an edit of their own record) or `actorAccountId` (a sign-up or readmission row sent from the member's app; under impersonation it is the ADMIN's). A refusal still returns `false`; nothing recorded, or a grant without such an author (the migration, a data operation, an ADMIN's D10 edit, an impersonation, a public sign-up sent before the account existed), returns `null`. No stored value is read differently anywhere else or changed: the census list, D10, the consent register and the data package keep showing the stored grant, and the value changes only when the member sends `imageConsent` (the web sends it only when the box is changed). `OnboardingState.imageConsent`'s OpenAPI description says this (snapshot regenerated; `docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md`).
- Tests: new `MemberOnboardingImageConsentTest` (3 unit cases: own grants by onboarding, by the ledger's `PROFILE` row and by an app sign-up row pre-tick; grants by an ADMIN, without author, migrated, public, impersonated, or an ADMIN's grant after the member's own, do not; a refusal is `false`, nothing recorded or another member `null`). `OnboardingIT`: new `PRIV_05_onlyAGrantGivenByTheMembersOwnAccountPreTicksTheImageBox` (over HTTP and MongoDB: an ADMIN's and an authorless grant read `null`, a refusal `false`; the member ticks it at onboarding and it reads `true`; ledger rows from the app read `true`, public and impersonated ones `null`; an ADMIN's later grant `null`, the rows kept). Changed: `OnboardingIT.Word_20261030_existingImageConsentIsReadAndPreservedWithoutANewChoice` asserted that a stored grant without author reads `true`; it now asserts `null` (unticked) with the grant still stored after an acceptance without a choice. Without the change the new cases fail (the service returned the stored `granted`). Run and passing: `MemberOnboardingImageConsentTest` (3), `ConsentLedgersTest`; ITs `OnboardingIT` (28), `IdentityContractIT` (11), `OpenApiSnapshotTest`.
- Assumption: «the member's own account ticked it before» is read on the grant in force (the latest image entry), not on any past entry: if staff recorded a grant after the member's own, the box starts unticked (the member's affirmative act is asked again; nothing stored changes unless they send it).
- Assumption: a grant from a public sign-up form is not pre-ticking: it was given before the member had an account, and the decision names the member's own account; the stored grant is kept and the member is shown an unticked box, which records nothing unless they change it.
- Assumption: `null` rather than `false` for a grant the member did not give, so that the API does not report a refusal that was never made; the web reads both as an unticked box (`state.imageConsent ?? false`).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S01-agilityhub-id-i-acces.md §14 (onboarding of imported and migrated accounts): the image box starts unticked unless the permission in force was given by the member's own account; values set by the migration, a data operation, the club or a public sign-up do not tick it; stored values are kept; §6 `GET /me/onboarding` row (`imageConsent`); Canvis.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/openapi/openapi.json: `OnboardingState.imageConsent` description.
- No web change is needed for the decision: `packages/auth/src/onboarding.tsx` already starts the box from `state.imageConsent ?? false` and sends `imageConsent` only when it is changed, which keeps the stored values.
