### PRIV-11 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `AesGcmCipher` writes Base64 of `nonce ‖ AES-GCM output` with no key identifier and reads with a single key (`AesGcmCipher.java:36-58`). `BankAccountVault` takes that key only from `BILLING_BANK_KEY` and stops startup without it outside `local`/`test` (`BankAccountVault.java:33-41`). Provider secrets use the same format under `BILLING_SECRETS_KEY` (`ProviderSecretVault.java:17`), which can be rotated by re-entering every club's secrets (`docs/DEPLOY.md:361-365`); the IBANs cannot. `docs/DEPLOY.md:341-352` allows a bank-key rotation only with a reviewed re-encryption, and none exists (`census:seal-ibans` only seals IBANs stored in clear).
- To decide: (a) build a re-key now: a key-id prefix on new ciphertexts, reads that still accept today's unprefixed values, a variable for the previous key, and a `bin/core` command that re-encrypts every stored IBAN (dry run, then `--apply`, idempotent); (b) schedule it for the first weeks after launch, as the auditor suggests; (c) keep one key per vault and treat a leak as a reviewed manual migration.
- NOT-CODE, before the first live remittance: keep a copy of `BILLING_BANK_KEY` and `BILLING_SECRETS_KEY` in a second, independent place (not the laptop or the database backup), and note where in the private runbook.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): build the IBAN re-key now: a key-id prefix on new ciphertexts, reads that still accept today's unprefixed values, an environment variable for the previous key, and a `bin/core` command that re-encrypts every stored IBAN (dry run by default, then `--apply`; idempotent; counts in the output; no IBAN in logs); the rotation procedure documented in docs/.
- Key id and format: `AesGcmCipher.keyId()` is the first 8 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the key's 32 bytes (`printf %s "$KEY" | base64 -d | sha256sum | cut -c1-8`). `BankAccountVault.encrypt` (hence `seal`, the signup, D10, the demo seeds and `migration:apply`) now stores `{keyId}:{Base64}`; a Base64 ciphertext never contains `:`. `decrypt` reads a prefixed value with the key its id names (the current one or the previous one; an unknown id fails with the usual `Bank account decryption failed`, never naming the value or a key) and a value without prefix (every value sealed before this change) with the current key, then the previous one. The associated data (`{clubId}:{memberId}`) is unchanged, so a value stays bound to its member.
- Previous key: `BILLING_BANK_KEY_PREVIOUS` (`core.billing.bank-key-previous` in `application.yml`, `.env.example`, `deploy/.env.prod.example`, forwarded by `deploy/compose.prod.yaml` with an empty default). It only reads, never seals. When set, in any profile, it must be Base64 of 32 bytes and another key than `BILLING_BANK_KEY` (and `BILLING_BANK_KEY` must be usable), or Core does not start.
- Command: `bin/core census:rekey-ibans [--dry-run | --apply]` (`RekeyIbansCommand`, rows from the new `SealedIbanRows`: the same four places as `census:seal-ibans`, i.e. the payment method flat or under `sepa` and a pending readmission's). It needs `BILLING_BANK_KEY`, prints `Current key: {id}, previous key: {id|none}`, then per member `WOULD_REKEY`/`REKEYED`/`SKIPPED`/`UNREADABLE` with member id, club and places only, and the counts (`Sealed IBANs`, `already with the current key`, `to re-encrypt` or `re-encrypted` and `skipped`, `unreadable`). Dry run by default (writes nothing). `--apply` writes each member with one update conditioned on the values read (a member edited meanwhile is `SKIPPED`; a rerun takes it). A value already sealed with the current key is left as it is, so a second `--apply` writes nothing. A value no configured key reads is listed `UNREADABLE`, left untouched, and the command exits with an error after printing the counts. The re-encryption happens inside the vault (`BankAccounts.reseal`): no IBAN or ciphertext is printed or logged. IBANs still in clear stay `census:seal-ibans`' business.
- Docs: `docs/DEPLOY.md` «Keys and provider rotation» has the 7-step rotation procedure (backup and escrow, `census:seal-ibans` at 0, set the previous and the new key and restart, dry run with `unreadable: 0`, `--apply` until 0, empty the previous key, keep the old key in escrow while any backup taken before the re-key is retained) and the environment table rows; `docs/MODEL_DADES_PLATAFORMA.md` (Member `paymentMethod.ibanEncrypted` format).
- Tests: `BankAccountVaultTest` +3 cases (a new value carries its key id and a value sealed before is still read; during a rotation the previous key reads its values, with or without id, never seals, and `reseal` moves them to the new key, which alone reads them afterwards; a malformed or repeated previous key stops the application in `prod` and `local`). New `RekeyIbansCommandIT` (5 cases against MongoDB: dry run writes nothing and apply re-encrypts flat, `sepa` and readmission values once, with `ibanLast4`, holder and mandate untouched, the IBAN in clear left alone, and a second apply writing nothing; without a previous key the values sealed before only gain the id; an unreadable value is reported, left as it is, and the command fails after re-encrypting the others; no key or bad arguments refuse and write nothing; a member changed meanwhile is skipped and taken by a rerun). Without the change none of them compiles (no `keyId`, `reseal`, `RekeyIbansCommand`). `PlayoffMigrationIT` was changed (it decoded the stored value as bare Base64; it now asserts the `{keyId}:` prefix and decodes what follows). Run and passing: `BankAccountVaultTest` (9), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `SepaRemittanceWriterTest` (25), `PlayoffAdapterTest` (11), `LogPrivacyTest` (6); ITs `RekeyIbansCommandIT` (5), `SealIbansCommandIT` (3), `PlayoffMigrationIT` (53), `CensusIT`.
- Assumption: the key id is derived from the key (a hash prefix) rather than configured by hand, so it can never disagree with the key and needs no new variable; 32 bits of SHA-256 of a random 256-bit key reveal nothing usable about it.
- Assumption: a value without prefix is tried with the current key first, then the previous one; nothing is rewritten on read. Adding the id to the values sealed before is optional (`census:rekey-ibans --apply` without a previous key does it), since they stay readable.
- Assumption: the command, like `census:seal-ibans`, writes no audit entry (an operator maintenance command; the stored IBAN and every audited field are unchanged, only their encryption).
- Assumption: the club's own creditor IBAN is a provider setting sealed with `BILLING_SECRETS_KEY` (`ProviderSecretVault`), not with the bank key: outside this re-key, as the docs say.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S03-cens-abonats-gossos.md R-03-07: `ibanEncrypted` starts with the id of the key that sealed it (`{keyId}:`); unprefixed values are still read; `BILLING_BANK_KEY_PREVIOUS` during a rotation; `bin/core census:rekey-ibans` (dry run by default, `--apply`, idempotent, counts, no IBAN) re-seals every stored IBAN with the current key; Canvis line.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/MODEL_DADES_PLATAFORMA.md Member `paymentMethod`: `ibanEncrypted` = `{keyId}:` + ciphertext since PRIV-11, re-sealed by `census:rekey-ibans`.
- No route, error code, parameter, event, notification or audit action changes; no OpenAPI change.
- Review note (review of p2-api-08-keys-privacy, 14 Nov 2026): accepted. The format change is one-way for older releases: once this release has sealed any IBAN (sign-up, D10, readmission, `migration:apply`), a release built before PRIV-11 cannot read it (it decodes the whole value as Base64 and fails on the `{keyId}:` prefix), so the SEPA file and D10's "same account" check fail for those members. Rolling production back past this commit after go-live therefore needs a forward fix instead (or a manual removal of the prefix from the values sealed meanwhile). The values stored today keep working unchanged on this release. Reviewed: the prefix is read only by `BankAccountVault`; every place a sealed IBAN is stored (the four `ClearIbanRows.PLACES` of `members`) is covered by the command; writes are conditioned on the value read; no IBAN or ciphertext reaches the output or the error messages; `RekeyIbansCommandIT` (5), `SealIbansCommandIT` (3), `PlayoffMigrationIT` (53), `CensusIT` (73), `SignupSecurityFixesIT` (54), `MigrationStagedImportIT` (12), `SupportAccessIT` (10) and `BankAccountVaultTest` (9) pass.

## api-08-platform-infra

### CFG-03 — FIXED
- The three anonymous webhooks no longer bind `@RequestBody byte[]`: `StripeWebhookController`, `BrevoWebhookController` and `SendGridWebhookController` read the body with `readNBytes(MAX_BODY_BYTES + 1)` (1 MiB, the cap Brevo and keyed requests already had), so no body is buffered beyond 1 MiB + 1 byte. A longer body → `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` before the Stripe/SendGrid signature (Brevo keeps its order: secret first, then the cap); an empty or unreadable body → 400, as Spring's required body did. The OpenAPI request-body annotation moved from the parameter to the method (springdoc 3.0.3 builds both through `buildRequestBodyFromDoc`), so `docs/openapi/openapi.json` should not change; `OpenApiSnapshotTest` (IT) was not run.
- `deploy/Caddyfile`: `request_body /webhooks/* { max_size 1MiB }` in the `{$CORE_HOST}` block, the only site that proxies `/webhooks/*`.
- Tests: new `StripeWebhookBodyLimitTest` (3) and `EmailWebhookBodyLimitTest` (5), with the helper `support/StreamedBody` (a 64 MiB body produced while it is read; the tests assert only 1 MiB + 1 byte was read), plus `ArchitectureTest` and `AnonymousRateLimitsTest`: ran and passed. New IT case `SendGridWebhookIT.CFG_03_aSignedBodyAboveOneMebibyteIsRefusedBeforeItsSignatureAndOneAtTheCapIsProcessed`: not run (MongoDB). New static check `deploy/test_e11_t09.py::test_CFG_03_the_core_host_caps_webhook_bodies_before_the_proxy`: ran and passed; the `caddy validate` tests need Docker and were not run.
- Left, to decide: a default body cap for `/api/*` and `/oauth2/*` at the proxy (the auditor's 2 MB, exempting the signed upload PUTs bounded by `files.maxSizeMb`) needs an inventory of the routes that legitimately take larger bodies first; the JVM flags (`-XX:MaxRAMPercentage`, `-XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError`) and `mem_limit` go with the droplet sizing of CFG-09.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): a default request-body cap of 2 MB for /api/* and /oauth2/* at the proxy; the signed upload PUTs stay bounded by files.maxSizeMb; first list the routes that legitimately take larger bodies and exempt them.
- Inventory (every `@RequestBody`, raw-stream read and multipart use of `src/main/java`, and the specs' size rules): only two routes legitimately take more than 2 MB, both signed upload PUTs whose bodies are files, streamed and counted by the Core against the grant (`files.maxSizeMb`, `files.dogPhotoMaxMb` for a dog photo): `PUT /api/v1/attachments/uploads/{id}` (`AttachmentsController`; also the target of the course, theme-logo, activity-image and dog-photo upload URLs, all issued by `AttachmentService`) and `PUT /api/v1/signup/uploads` (`SignupFilesController`). There is no multipart or other raw-body route under `/api/` or `/oauth2/`. Every other body is JSON or a form far below the cap: the Core already reads at most 1 MiB of a keyed request and 64 KB of a public signup (`IdempotencyFilter`), a course model is at most 256 KB (S16), a Smarter source 512 KB and goes through the upload route, and `/oauth2/*` takes short forms and `{flow}`. With S3 storage the uploads go to the bucket, not through Caddy.
- Code: `deploy/Caddyfile` new snippet `(api_body_limit)`: `request_body @api_body { max_size 2MB }` with `@api_body` = path `/api/*` `/oauth2/*` and not (`PUT` and path `/api/v1/attachments/uploads/*` `/api/v1/signup/uploads`), plus `handle_errors 413`, which answers the API's own refusal of an oversized body: `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` in the standard envelope (`{code, message, details: {}, traceId}`, Caddy's error id as `traceId`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, nosniff). Imported by `spa` (the id, clubs and clubs-admin product hosts), the `https://` club catch-all and the `{$CORE_HOST}` site (before its pass-1 webhook cap, which therefore answers the same envelope instead of Caddy's empty 413). `/.well-known/*` and `/connect/logout` (GETs) are not capped, as decided.
- Tests added: `deploy/test_e11_t09.py::ClubAppTest.test_CFG_03_every_site_that_proxies_the_api_caps_its_bodies_at_two_megabytes_but_the_signed_uploads` (static: the matcher, the exemptions, the cap, the answer, the only two `request_body` directives, and every site that proxies `/api/*` imports the snippet) and `BodyLimitTest.test_CFG_03_real_caddy_stops_api_bodies_above_two_megabytes_and_lets_the_signed_uploads_through` (the production snippets in the real `caddy:2.10.2-alpine`, host network, in front of a fictional upstream that reads and counts every body, on a `spa` site and on the Core host's block: 2,000,000 bytes pass and 2,000,001 are refused with the envelope, also chunked, for `/api/*` and `/oauth2/*`, and the upstream never receives the whole refused body; the two upload PUTs pass with 3 MB, a POST to the upload path, `PUT /attachments/upload-url`, `PUT /signup/uploads/extra` and another PUT are refused; the webhooks pass 1 MiB and refuse one byte more). Without the change the real-Caddy test fails (a 2,000,001-byte POST reaches the upstream; the webhook answer is an empty 413). No existing test changed: `test_CFG_03_the_core_host_caps_webhook_bodies_before_the_proxy` still holds.
- Run and passing (the Caddy image pulled from the configured registry mirror): `python3 deploy/test_e11_t09.py` (11), `deploy/test_gateway_unavailable.py` (9), `deploy/test_access_log.py` (3), `deploy/test_release_preflight.py` (30). `deploy/test_round2.py`: 14 of 15, `test_E11_T04_06_cron_executes_alert_for_failure_and_busy_lock` errors because the `agilityhub-backup:2` image is not built here (environment; that file is not in CI). No Java change, so no Maven run.
- Assumption: "2 MB" is Caddy's decimal `2MB`, 2,000,000 bytes (above every body the Core accepts outside the uploads). A refused body answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, the code and status the Core gives its own oversized bodies (CATALEG_ERRORS has no 413 row), so API clients get the standard envelope rather than Caddy's empty 413; this also applies to the Core host's 1 MiB webhook cap. The exempt uploads get no proxy ceiling of their own: the decision leaves them to `files.maxSizeMb`, a per-club parameter the proxy cannot read.
- Not in this decision: the JVM flags (`-XX:MaxRAMPercentage`, `-XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError`) and `mem_limit`, which stay with CFG-09's droplet sizing.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CONVENCIONS_API.md §9 (Seguretat bàsica): new rule «Mida del cos»: the proxy cuts `/api/*` and `/oauth2/*` bodies at 2 MB on every host and answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with the standard error body; only `PUT /api/v1/attachments/uploads/{id}` and `PUT /api/v1/signup/uploads` are exempt (bounded by `files.maxSizeMb`); webhooks keep 1 MiB with the same answer.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md «Security hardening (E11-T03)»: the body caps, their exemptions and the refusal.
- No route, schema, error code, parameter, event, notification or audit action changes; no OpenAPI change.
- OTHER-REPO: none. The web never sends a body near 2 MB outside the two upload PUTs; such a refusal would show its generic `errors:VALIDATION_ERROR` text.

### CFG-04 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `security_headers` (`deploy/Caddyfile:11-19`) sets nosniff, Referrer-Policy, X-Frame-Options and HSTS only, and its comment leaves the SPA CSP to the web build; the web repository defines none, and `apps/clubs/index.html:8-58` and `apps/clubs-admin/index.html:8-58` each carry an inline script (the cached-branding bootstrap). `CONVENCIONS_API.md:116` asks for "CSP per a les SPAs". API answers keep their own policy (`ContentSecurityPolicies`).
- To decide: (a) where the policy lives: a header in the static part of the Caddyfile (the `static_files` snippet of CFG-05, never on proxied API answers) or a `<meta http-equiv>` written by each app's build (no `frame-ancestors` there; `X-Frame-Options: DENY` covers it); (b) the inline script: its sha256 emitted at build time, or moved to a file; (c) `img-src`/`connect-src` need the attachment bucket's host once on S3, which differs per environment; (d) the rollout: `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` for some days, with or without a report endpoint, then enforced. A `Permissions-Policy` goes with it once the web confirms which browser features it uses.
- OTHER-REPO: web `apps/clubs/index.html:8-58` and `apps/clubs-admin/index.html:8-58`: the script's hash or its move to a file, and the policy itself if it goes in the build.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): the three web apps get a strict Content-Security-Policy sent by the server (Caddy), with the inline boot script allowed by its hash, computed at build time and checked by a test so that the two cannot drift.
- Web side (web FIXES.md CFG-04, already on the web branch): each app's `vite build` writes `content-security-policy.txt` into its `dist`, the header value on one line, with the `sha256` of every inline `<script>` of the built `index.html` (the cached-branding boot script of clubs and clubs-admin, none in id); `scripts/check-bundle-files.mjs` checks it in CI. This repository sends it and checks it again before a deploy, as that entry's OTHER-REPO asked.
- Code: `deploy/Caddyfile` `(static_files)` sets `Content-Security-Policy` to `{file.<root>/content-security-policy.txt}` for the root being served (`@policy_id`, `@policy_clubs`, `@policy_clubsadmin`: a `vars {http.vars.root}` matcher per bundle, so `spa`'s fixed roots and `club_spa`'s per-request choice of clubs or clubs-admin both send their own bundle's policy). Only static answers carry it: `api_proxy` and the Core host are untouched, so API answers keep `ContentSecurityPolicies.API`. Caddy reads the file on each answer (checked: a changed file is sent on the next request), so a new bundle brings its policy without a reload. The `security_headers` comment no longer leaves the CSP to the web build. `deploy/release-preflight.py` `check_dist` → new `check_policy`: refuses a dist whose `content-security-policy.txt` is missing (or a symlink), empty, holds a line break, or whose `script-src` lacks the `sha256` of an inline script of its `index.html` (`inline_script_hashes`, the same expressions as the web's `bundle-files.mjs`); the file is part of the dist fingerprint in the release record.
- Tests added: `deploy/test_gateway_unavailable.py::test_CFG_04_every_static_answer_carries_its_bundles_policy_and_the_api_keeps_its_own` (real Caddy: the fixture now mounts the three bundles with a policy each and a `spa` site for `/srv/id`, and the fictional Core's API answer carries `ContentSecurityPolicies.API`; `/`, a deep link, `/index.html`, an asset and a missing asset of the clubs and id product sites and of the club catch-all for a members and an office host each carry exactly their bundle's policy; `/api/v1/me` keeps only the API's; a rewritten policy file is sent on the next request). It fails on the previous Caddyfile (no header). `deploy/test_release_preflight.py::test_CFG_04_a_dist_needs_its_one_line_policy_and_it_must_allow_every_inline_script` (missing, empty, blank, trailing LF or CR, two lines; MDN's documented hash example as an independent vector; an inline boot script refused with no hash, another hash or the hash only in `default-src`, accepted with its hash in `script-src`, refused again once the script changes). It fails without the change. Changed fixture: the preflight test's `setUp` now gives each fictional dist a policy file (a production dist without one is now refused); no assertion was weakened.
- Run and passing: `deploy/test_gateway_unavailable.py` (10), `deploy/test_e11_t09.py` (11), `deploy/test_access_log.py` (3), `deploy/test_release_preflight.py` (31). No Java change, so no Maven run.
- Assumption: the policy text lives only in the web bundle, as the web's entry decided; this repository keeps no copy (so no fallback policy either). A dist without the file would get an empty header from Caddy, i.e. no policy, plus a Caddy error log line: the release preflight refuses such a dist, which is the guard. The preflight checks the file's shape and the hashes, not the directive list itself (the web's check compares it with its own policy).
- Assumption: the header is sent on every static answer of the three bundles (documents, assets, the service worker script, 404s), enforcing from the first deploy as the web's entry chose; Caddy's own 503 pages (F08) carry none (they hold no script). No `Permissions-Policy` (not decided). The local proof fixtures under `deploy/local/` have no policy file (local only; preflight refuses that directory anyway).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CONVENCIONS_API.md §9 (Seguretat bàsica): the SPAs' CSP rule as the web's copy has it (Caddy, static answers only, the value of `content-security-policy.txt`, its directives, the boot script's `sha256` computed at build time and checked by test), plus: Caddy reads the file from the served root on every answer and `bin/release-preflight` refuses a dist without it, with more than one line, or with an inline script its `script-src` does not allow.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md «Security hardening (E11-T03)» (the policy's source, the per-request read, the S3 origin caution) and the `bin/release-preflight` paragraph (the new refusal).

- OTHER-REPO: none left (the web's side is done). NOT-CODE: as the web's review note says, the policy allows only each host's own origin, so production must keep files in the private volume (`FILE_STORAGE_MODE=filesystem`) or the bucket's origin must be added to the web's policy first; and the staging pass with the header on (sign-in, branding, a dog photo, a receipt PDF, the 3D viewer, D9's e-mail preview, the push switch of 12) before production.
- Review note (review of p2-api-14-platform): `deploy/compose.prod.yaml` still defaults `FILE_STORAGE_MODE` to `s3` (also `.env.example`), and `bin/release-preflight` accepts an `s3` deployment with a policy that names no bucket origin. With that default the enforced policy (`connect-src`/`img-src` = `'self'`) would block the presigned uploads and the signed downloads and dog photos served from the bucket. Before the first deploy with this Caddyfile, confirm the production environment sets `FILE_STORAGE_MODE=filesystem` (with `deploy/compose.filesystem.yaml`), or have the web add the bucket's origin to its policy first; and do the staging pass listed above. The policy is enforced from the first deploy (no Report-Only period), as decided.

### CFG-06 — FIXED
- `deploy/compose.prod.yaml`: `stop_grace_period: 60s` on `core` and `120s` on `mongo` (mongod gets the SIGTERM through `mongo-entrypoint.sh`'s `exec`); `application.yml`: `spring.lifecycle.timeout-per-shutdown-phase: 45s` (graceful shutdown is already Boot 4.1's default; the phase's 30 s default was pre-empted by Docker's 10 s kill). `docs/DEPLOY.md` step 4 states it. Side effect: an open build-session SSE stream counts as a request in flight, so the old Core then stops after the full 45 s (before: killed at 10 s).
- Tests: `SentryPropertiesBindingTest` and `BankAccountVaultTest` (they load the real `application.yml`): ran and passed. The compose value has no test here (the compose-reading tests need Docker).
- Left, to decide: a release-tool guard that raises the maintenance flag and waits for, or refuses while, a job lease, billing run, remittance, card-charge batch or export is running (which operations block a deploy, and for how long); closing the build-session streams at the start of shutdown so they do not hold it. NOT-CODE: the billing owner confirms that a billing run cut mid-way resumes without double effects; until a guard exists, deploy outside billing and remittance windows.
- Review note: Compose stops the old Core before it starts the new one, and a stopping Core accepts no new connections, so while a build-session stream is open a deploy is unavailable for up to 45 s plus start-up (before: at most 10 s plus start-up). Values as the auditor proposed; the owner may prefer a shorter phase timeout or closing the streams first (above).

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): Core closes the live build-session streams at the start of shutdown so that they do not hold a deploy for the whole grace period.
- Code: `courses/api/BuildSessionStreams` is now also a `SmartLifecycle` (auto-started) with `PHASE = WebServerApplicationContext.GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_PHASE + 1`: on context close the lifecycle processor stops it before the web server's graceful shutdown starts waiting for the requests in flight. `stop()` marks the streams stopping and closes every live stream (`Subscriber.close`: what it has queued is sent, then the response completes; no `finished` event, since the session goes on) and logs the count; `subscribe` re-reads the flag after listing a new subscriber, so a stream opened while stopping ends at once (before its `:open`) and none can slip in after the stop's sweep. `start()` clears the flag. `destroy()` is unchanged (it still closes whatever is left and stops the timers). The web's client (`packages/api-client/src/build-events.ts`) already reopens a closed stream with a fresh token after a capped back-off and reads the session again, so it reaches the new Core once it answers. Comments in `application.yml` (lifecycle) and the class Javadoc say so.
- Tests added: `BuildSessionStreamsTest.CFG_06_stoppingEndsEveryLiveStreamAndOneOpenedMeanwhileEndsAtOnce` (two live streams end on `stop()`, no `finished`, none left; one opened meanwhile ends at once with no frame; after `start()` a stream stays open) and `CFG_06_theStreamsStopBeforeTheServerWaitsForItsRequests` (auto-start; the phase is above Boot's graceful-shutdown phase); `BuildSessionsIT.CFG_06_theStartOfShutdownEndsTheLiveStreamsBeforeTheServerWaitsForThem` (real server: in the real context the bean is running and its phase is above that of the `webServerGracefulShutdown` lifecycle; `stop()` ends two real curl streams over Tomcat, one with `?access_token=` and one with the bearer header, cleanly (exit 0) in under 2 s; a stream opened meanwhile gets its 200 and ends at once; the session is still `NOT_STARTED` and no `BuildSessionFinished` was published; after `start()` a stream opens again). The unit and IT cases fail without the change (the lifecycle methods do not exist; before, a stream ended only at `destroy()`, after the 45 s wait).
- Run and passing: unit `BuildSessionStreamsTest` (58, its `@RepeatedTest` included), `ArchitectureTest` (23), `E9ResponseContractTest` (15, it reads S16), `SentryPropertiesBindingTest` (2) and `BankAccountVaultTest` (9) (they load `application.yml`); IT `BuildSessionsIT` (19).
- Assumption: «at the start of shutdown» is the first lifecycle phase that stops before the server's graceful wait (the decision's purpose), rather than a `ContextClosedEvent` listener, which a child context's close (the management server's) could also trigger. The IT calls the lifecycle's `stop()` on the shared test context instead of closing it (closing would break the other tests of the context); the ordering against the real `webServerGracefulShutdown` bean is asserted on the real context. No other long request (exports, billing, job triggers) is cut: the decision names only the streams.
- NOT-CODE (as decided): «no deploys during billing or remittance windows» stays an operating rule; the release-tool guard of pass 1's «Left» is not built.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S16-recorreguts-rings-i-muntatge.md R-16-11: when the api stops (a deploy) the open SSE streams are closed at the start of the stop, without `finished`, and a stream opened meanwhile closes at once; the client reopens it with a new token and reads the session again. Canvis line 14-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md step 4 (the stop note): the build-session streams no longer hold the 45 s phase.
- No route, schema, error code, parameter, event, notification or audit action changes; no OpenAPI change (the operation's contract is unchanged: a stream may end at any time and the client reconnects).
- OTHER-REPO: none (the web already reopens a stream the server ends).

### CFG-07 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. The `(access_log)` snippet exists and only deletes `access_token` from the logged query (`deploy/Caddyfile:21-35`); no site imports it, by the decision in `docs/DEPLOY.md:398-399` ("Caddy access logging is off: query strings can contain signed capabilities"), which `deploy/test_access_log.py:26` enforces. Container logs are json-file, 5 × 10 MB per service, on the host only (`deploy/compose.prod.yaml:3-7`); `RequestTraceFilter` logs no requests.
- To decide (privacy and operations): whether the five sites log requests, and if so (a) drop the whole query string (a regexp filter on `request>uri` removing `\?.*`), since signed file URLs, OAuth codes and handoff codes travel there too, rather than delete named parameters; (b) the retention of the IP addresses (30, 60 or 90 days), recorded as personal-data processing; (c) where they go: a rolled file in a volume, or shipped off the host to a provider; (d) a larger or shipped container log for core.
- NOT-CODE: meanwhile the security trail is the `SecurityEvent` and audit collections in Mongo, which the backups keep.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): HTTP access log on for every site, with the query string dropped (it carries codes and signatures); sensible rotation.
- Code: `deploy/Caddyfile` `(access_log)` rewritten: JSON (`format filter`, `wrap json`) to `output file /var/log/caddy/access.log` with `roll_size 50MiB`, `roll_keep 30`, `roll_keep_for 30d` (rolled files gzip-compressed, Caddy's default); the filter drops the whole query string (`regexp \?.*$ ""`) from `request>uri`, from the `Referer` request header and from a `Location` answer (a signed download redirect, an OAuth redirect), instead of deleting `access_token` alone. Imported by `spa` (the id, clubs and clubs-admin product hosts), the `{$CORE_HOST}` site and the `https://` club catch-all. Caddy keeps redacting `Authorization`, `Cookie` and `Set-Cookie` (checked). The access loggers are excluded from Caddy's default log, so the container's json-file log is unchanged. `deploy/compose.prod.yaml`: a named volume `caddy-logs` at `/var/log/caddy` on `caddy`. The internal ask listener (`http://127.0.0.1:9080`) is not logged.
- Tests added: `deploy/test_access_log.py::test_CFG_07_every_public_site_imports_the_rolled_log` (static: the file and its rolling, the three importers, not the ask listener, the compose volume), `test_CFG_07_every_public_host_and_the_club_catch_all_write_the_rolled_file` (Caddy's own `caddy adapt` of the production Caddyfile: the four product and Core hosts have their own logger and the catch-all a default one, all five write the one file with 50 MiB / 30 files / 30 days through the three filters, all are kept out of the default log; the ask server has no logs), `test_CFG_07_real_caddy_logs_each_request_without_queries_or_credentials` (the snippet in the real image: an OAuth callback with a Referer that carries a handoff code, a redirect to a signed file URL, a token call with a bearer, a refresh cookie and a `Set-Cookie`, and another host; four JSON lines with address, host, method, status and time; no `?` and none of the fictional codes, signatures or credentials anywhere in the file; Referer and Location without their query; credentials `REDACTED`; read with `docker exec`, so no root-owned file is left on a CI runner).
- Changed tests (they asserted the old behaviour): `test_access_log.py::test_E9_T06_no_site_logs_without_the_filter` asserted that no site imported the snippet and that its filter deleted only `access_token`; it now asserts the single `log` directive is still the snippet's and that it drops the query of the URI, Referer and Location (the import is asserted by the new tests). `test_E9_T06_real_caddy_drops_the_query_token` read the line from the container's stderr and expected `?lastEventId=3` to stay; it now reads the file and expects the bare path. `test_e11_t09.py::test_CFG_05_the_core_host_…` (added under CFG-05) now copies the `(access_log)` snippet too, since the Core host's block imports it.
- Run and passing: `deploy/test_access_log.py` (6; the five CFG-07/E9-T06 assertions fail on the previous Caddyfile), `deploy/test_gateway_unavailable.py` (11), `deploy/test_e11_t09.py` (12), `deploy/test_release_preflight.py` (32); `deploy/test_round2.py` 14 of 15 (the backup-image environment error noted under CFG-03). No Java change, so no Maven run.
- Assumption: «every site» is every public site block (the three product hosts, the Core host and the club catch-all, which covers every club alias); the internal on-demand-TLS ask listener is Caddy calling itself on loopback, never published, so it is not logged. The query string is dropped from every logged URL, not only the request line: a same-origin `Referer` and a redirect's `Location` carry the same codes and signed URLs. Request headers otherwise stay (browser, origin), as Caddy logs them.
- Assumption: «sensible rotation» = a new file every 50 MiB, at most 30 rolled files, none older than 30 days: about a month of client addresses (personal data), the short end of the auditor's 30-90 days, enough for a 72-hour breach assessment; the disk use is bounded (50 MiB live plus 30 compressed files). Caddy rolls by size only, so under very light traffic the live file can hold lines older than 30 days. Logs stay on the host, in their own volume (shipping them off the host was not decided). The container logs keep Docker's 5 × 10 MB.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CONVENCIONS_API.md §9 (Seguretat bàsica): new rule «registre d'accés HTTP»: every public site logs each request in JSON to its own file, never to the container log, without the query string of the URI, `Referer` or `Location`, credentials hidden, rolled at 50 MiB, at most 30 files, none older than 30 days.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S14-tauler-auditoria-exportacions-rgpd.md R-14-16 (retenció): the access log keeps IP address, host, path without query, status and browser for at most 30 days, is not rewritten by an erasure (it expires on its own) and the privacy policy must say so; Canvis line 14-11-2026.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md «Logs and D+7 support»: the access log is on (what it holds, what it drops, where it is, rotation and retention, how to read it), replacing «Caddy access logging is off».
- No route, schema, error code, parameter, event, notification or audit action changes; no OpenAPI change.
- OTHER-REPO: web `docs/legal/POLITICA_PRIVACITAT_AGILITYHUB_ID.md` (ca/es/en) and the `apps/id` `/privacy` page text: the server's access log is new personal-data processing the policy does not list yet: add to «Seguretat i registre de canvis» that each request to the platform is recorded with the IP address, the browser, the date and the address requested (without its parameters), and to the retention section that these records are kept at most 30 days and are not modified (they expire, like the backups); add «30 days of access logs (api `deploy/Caddyfile`)» to the note of retention values at the top of the legal document. Whether that needs a new policy version is the web's call (the text has never been in effect). NOT-CODE: record the access log (purpose security, base legitimate interest, 30 days) in the club's record of processing activities.
- Review correction (review of p2-api-14-platform): Caddy redacts only `Authorization`, `Cookie` and `Set-Cookie`, so the new log wrote the two other credential headers of the API in clear: the anonymous signup's `X-Signup-Token` (the signup capability, sent by the checkout return of the clubs app, S04 R-04-26) and a club website's `X-Api-Key` (S17 R-17-11), shown by the new test against the pass-2 snippet. `deploy/Caddyfile` `(access_log)` now deletes `request>headers>X-Signup-Token` and `request>headers>X-Api-Key`. Test added: `deploy/test_access_log.py::test_CFG_07_real_caddy_keeps_the_capability_headers_out_of_the_log` (real Caddy: neither fictional value nor header reaches the file, the other headers stay; it fails on the pass-2 snippet). Changed test: `test_CFG_07_every_public_host_and_the_club_catch_all_write_the_rolled_file` asserted the exact set of three filtered fields; it now expects the five. Run and passing: `deploy/test_access_log.py` (7), `deploy/test_e11_t09.py` (12), `deploy/test_gateway_unavailable.py` (11), `deploy/test_release_preflight.py` (32); `deploy/test_round2.py` 14 of 15 (the backup-image environment error noted under CFG-03).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CONVENCIONS_API.md §9 «registre d'accés HTTP»: also without the credential headers `X-Signup-Token` and `X-Api-Key`. Also docs/DEPLOY.md «Logs and D+7 support».
- Review note: the access log is new processing of client addresses: the privacy-policy text (OTHER-REPO above) and the club's record of processing activities should be updated before the first deploy that carries this Caddyfile, not after. A future credential header must be added to the snippet's delete list.

### CFG-12 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `spring.profiles.default: local` (`application.yml:74-75`); under `local` the test clock and DNS routes are open (`SecurityConfiguration.java:73-80`) and provider fakes are used. Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): the image starts on the prod profile when no profile is given (`ENV SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE=prod` in the runtime stage); local and smoke runs already name their profile.

### CFG-14 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. `RateLimitFilter.route` (`RateLimitFilter.java:106-140`) gives buckets to anonymous, token, signup, webhook, signed-file, `/me` and `/platform` routes and `null` to every other authenticated route, as `CONVENCIONS_API.md:116` prescribes ("Rate limit per IP i per compte a `ANON` i a `/oauth2/token`"). Each call is bounded (page sizes, query deadlines, export row caps and lock).
- To decide (a CONVENCIONS_API change): a per-account default bucket for every authenticated route (the auditor suggests 600/min), and whether booking and waiting-list writes get a tighter one (fairness for popular slots is the club's call); the values, and whether they become catalogue parameters.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): a per-account bucket of 600 requests per minute on every authenticated API route (429 with the standard error body when exceeded); webhooks, health and the token endpoint keep their own limits.
- Code: `RateLimitFilter.route` answers `Route.ME` for an authenticated request to any `/api/` path that no other family claims (before: `null`, no limit). `ME` is the per-account bucket `/me` and `/platform/**` already had: keyed by the token's subject (the account) across addresses, capacity `core.security.rate-limits.me` = 600 per minute (configuration unchanged; comment in `application.yml`). So one bucket per account covers every authenticated API route. A refusal is the existing one: `429 RATE_LIMITED` with the standard `ApiError` body, `Retry-After` in seconds, `Cache-Control: no-store` and a `SecurityEvent RATE_LIMITED {limit: ME}` with the account and its club. Unchanged: health (never limited), `OPTIONS`, `/webhooks/*` (per address), `/oauth2/token` and `/auth/magic-link` (TOKEN, TOKEN_REFRESH, TOKEN_ACCOUNT), `/auth/handoff`, the checkout status, signed files, `/branding`, `/public/*`, the public signup family, and the OAuth/OIDC endpoints outside `/api/` (`/oauth2/revoke`, `/oauth2/userinfo`, `/.well-known/*`, `/connect/logout`: per address); a request without a bearer is not counted here (the security chain answers 401). No route, schema, error or OpenAPI change: every operation already documents `429` with `Retry-After`.
- Tests added: `AnonymousRateLimitsTest.CFG_14_everyAuthenticatedApiRouteCountsInTheAccountsBucket` (with a bearer, club reads and writes, an export, checkout creation, `/me` and `/platform` take `ME`; without one, none; health, `OPTIONS`, webhooks, token, magic link, handoff, checkout status, branding, public, signup, signed files, `/oauth2/revoke` and a non-API path keep theirs; the first assertions fail without the change) and `CFG_14_anAccountsRequestsShareOneBucketAcrossRoutesAndAddresses` (capacity 2: two calls on two routes from two addresses spend it, `/me` and `/audit-entries` are then `429` with `Retry-After: 60` and `RATE_LIMITED {limit: ME}` events for the account, health and another account go on, an anonymous call is not counted, a minute later it refills); `RateLimitIT.CFG_14_everyAuthenticatedApiRouteSharesTheAccountsQuotaOfSixHundredPerMinute` (over HTTP with the configured buckets: 600 calls mixing club routes, `/me` children, two addresses and `GET /members`; the 601st, `POST /bookings` from a third address, is `429 RATE_LIMITED`, `Retry-After: 60`, event `{limit: ME}`; another account and health go on; a minute later the account is served again). No existing test changed.
- Run and passing: unit `AnonymousRateLimitsTest` (33), `RateLimitsTest` (5), `WelcomeRateLimitTest` (3), `AuthControllerSurvivorsTest` (4), `HealthControllerTest` (13), `ArchitectureTest` (23); ITs `RateLimitIT` (12), `SecurityEventHashIT` (2), `SecurityHardeningIT` (5), `SecurityInventoryIT` (4), `WelcomeRateLimitIT` (1), `TrustedProxyClientIpIT` (2), `UntrustedPeerClientIpIT` (2), and the eight signup ITs that run with rate limits on, in one JVM so that their shared context shares its buckets as on CI: `SignupCensusCorrectionsIT` (22), `SignupFollowUpFixesIT` (15), `SignupGateFixesIT` (26), `SignupPlansFollowUpIT` (12), `SignupSecurityFixesIT` (54) pass, none refused `RATE_LIMITED`. `SignupIT` (1 of 35), `SignupMinorFixesIT` (1 of 23) and `SignupPaymentMethodsIT` (4 of 10) fail with `403 RECENT_LOGIN_REQUIRED` on `PATCH /members/{id}` with a payment method: not this change. `SignupPaymentMethodsIT` fails the same 4 at `p2-start/p2-api-06-access-limits` (a8b2269, run from a clean export of that commit); these classes' ADMIN tokens carry no `auth_time` since AUTH-01's step-up (6cf572b, p2-api-05-access), whose list of adapted helpers does not name them. Left for that package's owner.
- Assumption: the decision's per-account bucket is the existing `ME` bucket (per account, 600/min, on `/me` and `/platform` already), extended to the other authenticated API routes, so an account has one 600/min quota for all of them rather than a second one on top. The routes that have a family of their own keep it: the decision names webhooks, health and the token endpoint, and the same reading is applied to `/auth/magic-link`, `/auth/handoff`, checkout status, signed files, `/branding`, `/public/*` and the signup family (anonymous by design or with a stricter limit) and to the OAuth endpoints outside `/api/`. The enum constant and the configuration key keep their name (`ME`, `me`) so that a deployed override (`CORE_SECURITY_RATELIMITS_ME_CAPACITY`) keeps working; a security event of a club route therefore reads `{limit: ME}`. No tighter bucket for booking and waiting-list writes (the auditor's suggestion, not decided). The value stays configuration, like every other bucket, not a catalogue parameter. Counters stay per process in memory (one instance, ADR-003).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CONVENCIONS_API.md §9 (Seguretat bàsica): every authenticated `/api/` route counts in the account's quota of 600 requests/min, shared across routes and addresses (the one `/me` and `/platform` had); `429 RATE_LIMITED` with the standard error body, `Retry-After` and a `SecurityEvent RATE_LIMITED`; webhooks, health, `/oauth2/token` and the routes with a quota of their own keep it. (Also docs/DEPLOY.md, the `core.security.rate-limits.me` row.)
- OTHER-REPO: none. The web already shows `errors:RATE_LIMITED` («Too many requests. Try again later.») for a 429 of any operation.

## api-09-messaging

### COMM-02 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. N-15 is MEMBER APP+SMS+PUSH with no EMAIL (`NotificationCatalog.java:153-156`), as `CATALEG_NOTIFICACIONS.md:32` and R-11-12 prescribe, so a club cannot switch e-mail on in the matrix. N-13 is OPERATIONAL, whose e-mail is off by default (`NotificationPreference.java:28`), and the preference applies to `mandatory` codes too (`ChannelResolver.java:64,68-70`). An SMS refused by the guard ends `SKIPPED_NOT_ALLOWED` with no fallback (`NotificationDispatcher.java:287-288`); the forced e-mail exists only at the monthly cap (R-11-06, `ChannelResolver.capReached`). The second auditor's corrections hold: the Cànic runs `ALL_AT_ONCE` (no confirm-by, nothing lapses), push with no VAPID key is `SKIPPED_NO_CONTACT`, and a member with an account always gets the APP copy, so `offerNotifiedAt` is set by design.
- To decide (catalogue and spec changes): (a) add EMAIL to N-15's caps and seed matrix (catalogue, `CATALEG_NOTIFICACIONS`, seed in one commit), or keep seat notices in-app and SMS only and tell members; (b) whether a `mandatory` code ignores the OPERATIONAL e-mail preference; (c) whether an SMS that ends `SKIPPED_NOT_ALLOWED` or `FAILED` forces an e-mail as the cap does (an R-11-06 extension).
- NOT-CODE: before L1, read `waitlist.mode` and the module list of the production club (`docs/DEPLOY.md:40-41` asks for SMS/PUSH off under hosted demo), and either have SMS live or accept in-app seat notices.
- OTHER-REPO (no API change): web `apps/clubs/src/profile/NoticesCard.tsx:164` should also require `branding.pushPublicKey` for `pushRow`, so the push switch is hidden while the server publishes no key (today `subscribeToPush` returns "unavailable" silently, `push.ts:161-175`). The reminder selector (:219-238) stays: a reminder also arrives in-app, and by e-mail when OPERATIONAL e-mail is on.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): the waiting-list «seat released» notice (N-15) and the class reminders (N-13) also go by e-mail, respecting the member's e-mail preferences and the unsubscribe rules.
- `NotificationCatalog`: N-15's MEMBER default channels are now APP + EMAIL + SMS (and PUSH, fixed by the code). EMAIL was already inside N-15's caps (OPERATIONAL gives APP + EMAIL, plus the member SMS exception); only its seed cell was off, so pass 1's «a club cannot switch e-mail on» was wrong: D9 could already tick it. The product seed `seed/message-templates.{ca,es,en}.json` turns N-15's MEMBER `EMAIL` cell on (`MessageTemplateSeed` refuses a seed whose matrix differs from the catalogue). N-13 needed no code change: its seed already had EMAIL on, under the OPERATIONAL e-mail preference.
- No change to `ChannelResolver`, the dispatcher or the e-mail layout: both codes' e-mail follows the member's `emailByCategory.OPERATIONAL` (product default off; screen 12's «Operativa» row, D10 for the admin), also for N-15, which is `mandatory`; the bounce and suppression checks run at every attempt; OPERATIONAL mail carries no unsubscribe footer nor `List-Unsubscribe` (R-11-08: CLUB_NEWS only). `BookingNotificationFacts.reachable()` already counts a QUEUED/SENT e-mail, so a waiting member with an address and «Operativa» e-mail on but no account or phone is now reached and marked (`offerNotifiedAt`).
- Tests added: unit `ChannelResolverTest.COMM_02_theSeatOfferAndTheReminderGoByEmailWhenTheOperationalPreferenceIsOn` (both seed matrices hold EMAIL; preference on → one QUEUED e-mail per address; default → `SKIPPED_BY_PREFERENCE`; N-15 mandatory and still skipped); IT `NotificationEngineIT.COMM_02_theSeatReleasedNoticeAndTheReminderAlsoGoByEmailUnderTheOperationalPreference` (`WaitlistNotified` to a member with the default preference: `EMAIL:SKIPPED_BY_PREFERENCE`, no mail; to one with «Operativa» on: `EMAIL:SENT`, subject = the title, no `List-Unsubscribe`; that member's `ReminderDue`: `EMAIL:SENT`); `WaitlistIT.T_08_20_allAtOnceNotifiesEveryWaitingMemberOnceTheFirstClaimWinsAndTheRestReturnToActiveWithN46` asserts the three N-15 now carry an EMAIL delivery, skipped by the default preference. Each fails without the change (no EMAIL delivery on N-15).
- Changed tests (they asserted the old channels): `NotificationSpecTest.WP_11_A_lookupsReturnTheRegisteredCodesAndOnlyR1CodesPerEvent` (N-15's default channels gain EMAIL); `WaitlistIT.T_08_20_anOfferWithNoN15RecipientIsNotMarkedAndGetsNoN46` (c0's one row that is not `SKIPPED_NO_CONTACT` is now its EMAIL skipped by preference, asserted exactly; still no mark); `NotificationActionsIT` SEAT_RELEASED (Pere's N-15 gains `EMAIL:SKIPPED_BY_PREFERENCE`, as his N-13 in the same suite). Test repair of PAY-06's counts in b171514 (see PAY-06).
- Run and passing: unit `ChannelResolverTest` (with its truth-table rows), `NotificationSpecTest`, `NotificationCatalogContractTest`, `MessageTemplateSeedTest`, `MessageTemplateSeedChecksTest`, `NotificationSeedSnapshotTest`, `NotificationMatrixTest`, `TemplateValidatorTest` (2,875 tests); ITs `NotificationEngineIT` (16), `WaitlistIT` (23), `NotificationActionsIT` (15), `BookingJobsIT` (3), `AttendanceIT` (22), `TemplateVariableParityIT` (3), `TemplateUpgradeIT` (7), `MessageTemplatesIT` (11), `E7ContractIT` (29).
- Assumption: «respecting the member's e-mail preferences» is the existing per-category preference. Both codes are OPERATIONAL, whose e-mail is off until the member turns «Operativa» on, so with the product defaults a member gets these e-mails only after turning it on; a mandatory code does not bypass the preference (pass 1's option (b) not taken) and no e-mail is forced when an SMS is refused or fails (option (c) was not decided).

- Assumption: a club's stored template keeps its channel matrix (ruling E81: the start-up `TemplateUpgrade` brings texts, never matrices, up to date; D9 is where a club chooses channels). A club seeded from now on, and «Restaura el text per defecte», get N-15's e-mail ticked; the Cànic's existing N-15 template keeps it off until an admin ticks it. For the owner (written in docs/DEPLOY.md): in production, D9 → «S'ha alliberat una plaça!» → tick «Correu» for «Alumne» and save.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_NOTIFICACIONS.md N-15 row: MEMBER → APP+SMS+PUSH (EMAIL segons preferència); rule 2: N-13 and N-15 also by e-mail under the OPERATIONAL preference, which a mandatory code does not bypass.
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/S11-comunicacions.md R-11-04 (the COMM-02 sentence: N-15 and N-13 by e-mail under `emailByCategory.OPERATIONAL`, no unsubscribe footer, in addition to SMS and push); §7 `WaitlistNotified` row (SMS + PUSH + EMAIL by preference); Canvis.

- OTHER-REPO: none required by this decision (no API change; D9 already shows and saves N-15's «Correu» cell, inside its caps).
- Review note: reviewed 48ca07e (and b171514, PAY-06's two catalogue counts it repaired, correct): the catalogue row, the three seed matrices, the resolver path, the spec copies and the tests match the decision as read, and the preference path is the existing one (no bounce, suppression or unsubscribe check bypassed). For the owner, a business point the code cannot settle: with this reading the decision changes nothing a member sees until two manual steps are taken. «Operativa» e-mail is off by default for every member, and the Cànic's stored N-15 template keeps its e-mail cell off (the start-up upgrade never changes a stored matrix), so in production neither the seat-released notice nor the reminder reaches anyone by e-mail until an admin ticks «Correu» for «Alumne» on «S'ha alliberat una plaça!» in D9 and each member turns «Operativa» on in screen 12 (or an admin in D10). If the intent was an e-mail fallback that works out of the box while SMS and push are not live, the default of that preference (or a per-code default) is a separate decision. Run here: unit `ChannelResolverTest`, `NotificationSpecTest`, `NotificationCatalogContractTest`, `MessageTemplateSeedTest`, `MessageTemplateSeedChecksTest`, `NotificationSeedSnapshotTest`, `NotificationMatrixTest`, `TemplateValidatorTest`; ITs `NotificationEngineIT` (16), `WaitlistIT` (23), `NotificationActionsIT` (15): all passing.

### COMM-05 — PARTIAL
- `PushSubscriptionService.validate` (new `pushServiceAddress`) also refuses, with `422 PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION_INVALID {field: endpoint}`, an `https` endpoint whose host is an IP literal (v4, v6 or a bare number), a single-label name (`localhost`, a Compose service such as `mongo`) or a `*.localhost` name, or whose port is not 443. Every browser push service (FCM, Mozilla, Apple, WNS) is a DNS name on 443, so no real device is refused; with https only and certificate checking, the sender can then reach only public HTTPS hosts on port 443. The controller's description, `docs/openapi/openapi.json` (that one string, edited by hand) and `docs/openapi/CHANGELOG.md` changed with it.
- Tests: new `MessagingE7T03UnitTest.COMM_05_anEndpointOnAnAddressOrPortNoPushServiceUsesIsRefused` ran and passed (with the class's 6 other tests). Four refused bodies added to `PushSubscriptionsIT.T_11_21_malformedEndpointsAndKeysAreRejected`: not run (MongoDB), nor was `OpenApiSnapshotTest`.
- Not re-checked: rows already in `push_subscriptions` (the dispatcher sends to the stored URL). Production runs hosted demo, whose push sender is a stub that calls nothing; before `WebPushSender` is enabled, list the ACTIVE rows whose endpoint the new check would refuse and expire them.
- Left, to decide: (a) an allow-list of push-service hosts (`fcm.googleapis.com`, `updates.push.services.mozilla.com`, `*.push.apple.com`, `*.notify.windows.com`): it fixes which browsers are supported and needs an exception, by profile or property, for the `push.example.test` fakes of the demo seed, `bin/e7-smoke` and the ITs; (b) a cap of active subscriptions per account (the value, and refusing the next one or expiring the oldest); (c) push on its own sender thread and a timeout shorter than today's 10 s per attempt (INC-40's single notification scheduler serves e-mail, SMS and push). Until (a) or (b) exists, a member can register many slow endpoints on a domain of their own and delay the club's sends once `WebPushSender` is live.
- Review note: the check is on the URL's host name only, so "the sender can then reach only public HTTPS hosts on port 443" holds for literal addresses but not for a public DNS name that resolves to a private or loopback address (a wildcard-DNS name, or a member's own domain pointed at `10.x`): the sender still opens a TCP/TLS connection to that internal address on 443. Certificate checking and the client's default `Redirect.NEVER` (`MessagingConfiguration.java:141`) keep the request body from being sent to a host without a valid certificate for that name, so what remains is a connect/timing probe. The allow-list in (a) closes it; a resolve-time check would not (DNS rebinding), so it was not added here.

### COMM-06 — FIXED
- Verified, no code touched. Brevo and Twilio adapters exist and the monthly cap works (`ClubSmsUsage.java:42-60`). There are no quiet hours in code or S11; one SMS goes to each stored phone, with no member preference (`ChannelResolver.java:72-76`, the fixed «+SMS» of screen 12); numbers are parsed with libphonenumber but their type is not checked (`CountryContactRules.java:29-36`). A provider 4xx ends `FAILED` with no alert; a timeout (an `IOException`) is retried with no idempotency key (`BrevoSmsSender.java:40-46`). «Envia prova» sends APP and e-mail only (`TemplatePreviewService.java:94-104`). `messaging.sms.senderId` defaults to `AgilityHub` (`catalog.yaml:875-877`).
- To decide: (a) quiet hours: which codes wait (an N-15 in FIFO cannot wait past `confirm_by`), the window, and a new parameter in the closed catalogue; (b) mobile numbers only (libphonenumber's number type, already a dependency) or every stored number, including `phone2`; (c) an admin notice on a FAILED SMS (a new catalogue notification, like N-49 at the cap) and the e-mail fallback of COMM-02 (c); (d) after a timeout, retry (possible duplicate SMS) or settle FAILED (possible lost SMS); (e) whether members may opt out of SMS (S11 shows it fixed); (f) a test SMS in «envia prova».
- NOT-CODE: a live proof on staff numbers first; register the sender id for Spain with the provider and set `messaging.sms.senderId` to the club's name; start with a low `messaging.sms.monthlyCap`; set a low-balance alert in the provider's console.

Pass 2 (owner decision of 13 Nov 2026): SMS quiet hours 22:00–08:00 in the club's time zone for non-urgent codes, which wait until 08:00, while seat offers with a deadline do not wait; SMS only to mobile numbers (Spanish 6/7 prefixes and valid E.164 mobiles), a landline being skipped and recorded.
- Two club parameters (AGENTS rule 3) in `parameters/catalog.yaml`: `messaging.sms.quietHoursStart` (time, 22:00) and `messaging.sms.quietHoursEnd` (time, 08:00), block «messaging», module `SMS`, editable by the club; the window may cross midnight, the start is inside and the end outside it, equal bounds mean no quiet hours.
- New `SmsQuietHours` (messaging.domain): `deferUntil(now, zone)` gives the window's next end in the club's zone (today's 08:00 at 03:00, tomorrow's at 23:00; wall-clock across a DST change) and `urgent(code, variables)` is true only for N-15 whose stored variables carry `confirm_by` (a FIFO offer).
- `NotificationDispatcher.sendSms`, before the monthly-cap reservation: a non-urgent SMS inside the window returns the new `Outcome.deferred(until, «SMS quiet hours»)`, settled by `deferred(...)`: the delivery stays `QUEUED` with `nextAttemptAt` = the window's end and that `lastError`, its attempts unchanged, its lease released; nothing reserved, sent or published. The 5-second poll claims it at 08:00 and sends it after the usual checks (a notice that no longer applies ends `SKIPPED_STALE`). APP, e-mail and push are never delayed. `Outcome.abandoned()` excludes a deferral.
- New `MobileNumbers` (messaging.domain): a `+34` number is a mobile when it is `+34` and nine digits starting with 6 or 7; any other must be valid for libphonenumber (already a dependency) and of type `MOBILE` or `FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE`. `ChannelResolver` plans one SMS per mobile and records every other phone as `Planned(SMS, number, SKIPPED_NO_CONTACT, «Not a mobile number»)` (new optional `reason` on `Planned`, a three-argument constructor kept; `NotificationEngine` writes it as the delivery's `lastError`). `sendSms` checks the target again at each attempt, so an SMS queued to a landline before this change, or by another path, is skipped the same way with no call and nothing counted.
- Tests added: unit `SmsQuietHoursTest` (4: the wait and its end in the club's zone, Madrid vs Buenos Aires, both 2026 DST changes, a window inside one day, equal or missing bounds, which SMS is urgent), `MobileNumbersTest` (2: Spanish 6/7, landlines 8/9, a 900 number, wrong lengths; French and British mobiles and landlines, a North American number, toll-free, malformed values), `ChannelResolverTest.T_11_06_COMM_06_aLandlineGetsNoSmsAndIsRecordedWhileTheMobileGetsIt`; ITs `NotificationDispatcherIT.T_11_06_COMM_06_quietHoursHoldANonUrgentSmsUntilTheirEndAndASeatOfferWithADeadlineGoesAtOnce` (23:30: N-08a's SMS `QUEUED` until 08:00 with no attempt, nothing sent or counted, no `NotificationSent{SMS}`, the e-mail sent at once; not due at 07:59, sent once at 08:00; a FIFO N-15 with `confirm_by` sent at 23:30 to both phones, an ALL_AT_ONCE one waits; a club that moves the window sends at 23:30) and `…aLandlineGetsNoSmsAndIsRecordedWhileTheMobileGetsIt` (the landline recorded with its number and the reason while the mobile gets the SMS; a delivery to a landline queued beforehand skipped at its attempt, no call, nothing counted). Each fails without the change.
- Changed tests (they asserted an SMS sent inside what are now quiet hours): `NotificationDispatcherIT.T_11_07_theMonthlyCapSkipsTheSmsForcesTheEmailAndNotifiesTheAdminsOncePerMonth` (00:30 in Madrid: the two SMS wait for 08:00, nothing counted, then go on November's counter), `T_11_15_theSmsCounterUsesEachClubsOwnMonth` (Madrid's SMS at 00:30 waits for 08:00, still November there; Buenos Aires's at 20:30 goes at once in October), `T_11_07_R_11_06_aSenderLateOnTheOldMonthNeverMovesTheCounterNorTheNoticeMarkerBack` (its club gets equal bounds, no quiet hours, because a month only changes at midnight, inside the default window; its assertions are unchanged), `NotificationActionsIT` CLASS_AT_RISK_AND_AUTO_CANCELLED (the 7:30 risk review's N-08a SMS to Joan is `QUEUED`, then sent at 8:00), `CalendarIT.T_06_14_T_06_15_T_06_34_cancellationRollsBackAndDeliversEveryDogInRecipientLocaleOnce` (at 06:00 local the 12 SMS wait with «SMS quiet hours», then all 12 go at 08:00).
- Run and passing: unit, every unit test class under `clubs/messaging` (36 classes, with `ChannelResolverTest`'s truth table), `ParameterCatalogContractTest`, `ArchitectureTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`, `ClockRuleTest`, `SeedAndWriterRulesTest`, `MessageParityTest` (3,121 tests); ITs `NotificationDispatcherIT` (26), `NotificationEngineIT` (16), `NotificationActionsIT` (15), `CalendarIT` (26), `ActivityIT` (36), `AttendanceIT` (22), `BookingsIT` (26), `WaitlistIT` (23), `WeekOpeningJobIT` (4), `TrainingIT` (33), `AnnouncementsIT` (5), `NotificationLogIT` (6), `NotificationPreferencesIT` (6), `NotificationFeedIT` (5), `E7PersistenceIT` (8), `E7ContractIT` (29), `E5ContractIT` (41), `MessageTemplatesIT` (11), `TemplateVariableParityIT` (3), `FollowupIT` (29), `SettingsIT` (23), `DemoPlanningSeedIT` (10), `ClubDefinitionsIT` (22: 21 pass, among them `T_02_12` against `fixtures/parameters-canic.json`, which gains the two keys; `T_17_01` fails as in it-baseline.txt, environment).
- Assumption: «non-urgent codes» are every SMS code but a seat offer with a deadline. N-08a, N-08b, N-32c, N-32d, N-36, N-47, an announcement sent with a `CUSTOM` CLUB_CHANGES template, and an N-15 without a deadline (ALL_AT_ONCE, the Cànic's mode) all wait. For the owner: a class cancelled or changed by the club at night, or cancelled by P2's 7:30 risk review, whose class starts before 08:00 gets its SMS at 08:00, after the start (its app and e-mail copies go at once); an ALL_AT_ONCE seat freed at night is texted at 08:00 if it is still offered (otherwise `SKIPPED_STALE`).
- Assumption: a deferred SMS keeps the end computed when it was deferred; a club that changes its window later does not move SMS already waiting. The window is club-local wall-clock time; instants are stored in UTC.
- Assumption: «valid E.164 mobiles» outside Spain are libphonenumber's `MOBILE` numbers and its `FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE` ones (North America and similar plans, where a landline cannot be told apart): only a number known not to be a mobile is skipped. Spanish numbers follow the owner's 6/7 rule, not libphonenumber's ranges (so `+3470…` counts as a mobile).
- Assumption: «skipped and recorded» uses the existing status `SKIPPED_NO_CONTACT` (rule 7: no usable contact), with the number as the delivery's target and «Not a mobile number» as its `lastError`, shown by the log's detail (`GET /notifications/{id}`); no new status in the closed catalogue, no notice to the admins.

- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/specs/00-transversal/CATALEG_PARAMETRES.md «Comunicacions»: new `messaging.sms.quietHoursStart` (time, 22:00) and `messaging.sms.quietHoursEnd` (time, 08:00).
- SPEC-CHANGE: docs/DEPLOY.md «Notifications» (mobiles only, the quiet hours, equal bounds) and E7 gate (run `bin/e7-smoke` in the daytime). No OpenAPI change (no route or schema changed).
